### SHIFT BILLING AND VALIDITY DATES
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/5821982-prepare-temporary-closing-of-your-studio-holiday-mode

When is the best time to prepare the temporary closure of your studio?
When is the best time to prepare the temporary closure of your studio?
The best time to prepare for the closing of your studio is as soon as possible.
Except for pausing your subscriptions which would be best done the day before closing.
In this article, you will see how :

## SHIFT BILLING AND VALIDITY DATES

SHIFT BILLING AND VALIDITY DATES
1. Extend the validity dates of your passes
1. Extend the validity dates of your passes
> Go to "Products" and then "Passes"
> For each pass, choose "EXTEND THE VALIDITY FOR ALL MEMBERS".
> Range of dates :
- The soonest the : Starting day of studio closure
The soonest the : Starting day of studio closure
- The latest the : Closing day of studio + Duration of closure
The latest the : Closing day of studio + Duration of closure
> Indicate the number of additional days corresponding to the duration of the closure
> Indicate a note explaining the closure
> Click on "VALIDATE" to save
For example :
- Holidays from 15/08/2022 to 31/08/2022
- Range of dates :
- The soonest the : 15/08/2022
The soonest the : 15/08/2022
- The latest the :  31/08/2022 + 17 days = 17/09/2022
The latest the :  31/08/2022 + 17 days = 17/09/2022
- Number of additional days: 17 days

## 2. Shifting the billing date of subscriptions

2. Shifting the billing date of subscriptions
To shift the billing date of the subscriptions, you need to add a break in the number of days of closure:
> Go to "Products" then "Subscriptions"
> For each contract, choose "PAUSE"
> Add the reason for the closure
> Enter the dates of your studio's closure
- start date (included)
start date (included)
- end date (included)
end date (included)
> Click on "VERIFY" to save

## ⚠️ PLEASE NOTE - special cases :

⚠️ PLEASE NOTE - special cases :
- Subscriptions which the very first invoice starts during the date interval
Subscriptions which the very first invoice starts during the date interval
Subscriptions which the very first invoice starts during the date interval
If a subscription starts during the date range you specify, the very first invoice will be shifted to the end of the break.
Example:
If you close your studio from December 15 to January 1 and a subscription begins on December 20, the subscription start date will be shifted to January 2.
- Subscriptions created after the pause creation
Subscriptions created after the pause creation
Subscriptions created after the pause creation
All the subscriptions that will be created after the pause has been created have to be manually paused.
To avoid this, we advise you to make subscriptions "unavailable for purchase" between the beginning of the interval and the end of your holiday.
For example :
Make your subscriptions "unavailable for purchase" between 31/07/2022 and 31/08/2022.
- Subscriptions with invoices invoiced on the day when I set up my pause
Subscriptions with invoices invoiced on the day when I set up my pause
Subscriptions with invoices invoiced on the day when I set up my pause
In this case all those subscriptions won’t be taken into account and no pause will be applied. The list will be indicated when creating the pause and you’ll have to wait for the day after to manually pause each of the subscriptions.

### Intercom
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/7185802-allow-your-practitioners-to-rent-a-room-for-their-consultations

The idea is to set up private appointments that the member can book, as well as payroll rules that will enable you to track what your practitioners owe you for any room rental.
Contrary to their original use, the payroll rules will allow you to know exactly how many hours the practitioner has used your room. With this information, you can invoice them for the hours they owe you.

#### All you need to do is follow three simple steps:

All you need to do is follow three simple steps:
- Create private appointments and set-up the availabilities of practitioners (created in teacher's section) from their teacher account, in order to offer slots that can be booked by members.​​> To help you set up your private appointments, please refer to this article! ​​
Create private appointments and set-up the availabilities of practitioners (created in teacher's section) from their teacher account, in order to offer slots that can be booked by members.​​> To help you set up your private appointments, please refer to this article! ​​
> To help you set up your private appointments, please refer to this article!
- Set the payroll rules that will determine how much practitioners are billed for each of their consultations, for the rental of your room. ​Here, the aim is to set a fixed rate for each consultation: if a member books a one-hour slot, the teacher will have to pay you the amount of his fixed "remuneration". ​​Here's an example:  Room rental costs the practitioner €50, so I set my payroll rule so that it "pays" (which, in reality, will be billed to the teacher) a fixed rate of €50 per consultation.​A member books a consultation: this blocks a slot. I find the consultation on the teacher's pay slip. I now know that the practitioner owes me €50 for the room rental he used to carry out his consultation with the member.​
Set the payroll rules that will determine how much practitioners are billed for each of their consultations, for the rental of your room. ​Here, the aim is to set a fixed rate for each consultation: if a member books a one-hour slot, the teacher will have to pay you the amount of his fixed "remuneration". ​​Here's an example:
Here's an example:
Room rental costs the practitioner €50, so I set my payroll rule so that it "pays" (which, in reality, will be billed to the teacher) a fixed rate of €50 per consultation.​
A member books a consultation: this blocks a slot. I find the consultation on the teacher's pay slip. I now know that the practitioner owes me €50 for the room rental he used to carry out his consultation with the member.​
Don't forget billing! Two options here:
Don't forget billing! Two options here:
a. Give members the option of paying their appointment card later, and keep all appointments unpaid until the teacher collects the person and registers them on the platform as "Manual Payment".
"Manual Payment"
⚠️ You will also need to give your practitioners/staff access so that they can manually update the invoice for their consultations/sessions once the member has done his session.
OR​b. Set the appointment card to 0€, so that all your members can book easily and can only pay the practitioner when they arrive at the consultation.
⚠️ The practitioner will not have to manually enter the payment in the back-office. The downside is that there will be no real accounting follow-up, as appointment cards will be invoiced at 0€.

## For further information on how to manage your private appointments, go to the Appointments section of the Help Center.

### Intercom
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/14210031-how-to-configure-passes-in-bsport-for-frequent-use-cases

Managing passes effectively in bsport is crucial for ensuring smooth operations and a seamless client experience. This article addresses frequently asked questions related to pass credits, compatibility, and booking, along with actionable steps to resolve them.

## Pass Credits Usage and Compatibility

## Why did a client’s purchased credits not appear when they try to book a class?

If a client’s purchased credits do not appear, consider the following steps:
- Check for multiple accounts: Verify whether the client has multiple accounts. The purchase and credits might be linked to a different account. Consolidating the correct client account can resolve this issue. It is possible that the client has two separate email addresses registered with the studio. The pass may be assigned to one but not the other.
Check for multiple accounts: Verify whether the client has multiple accounts. The purchase and credits might be linked to a different account. Consolidating the correct client account can resolve this issue. It is possible that the client has two separate email addresses registered with the studio. The pass may be assigned to one but not the other.
Check for multiple accounts
- Check the validity of the pass: If the pass is not appearing it could be expired. You can check the validity of a pass on the members account by searching the members
Check the validity of the pass: If the pass is not appearing it could be expired. You can check the validity of a pass on the members account by searching the members
Check the validity of the pass:
- Check the compatibility of the pass:You can do this through Classes > Group Activities > Select the activity. You will then see the section for "Compatible Passes" at the top. All the passes that are compatible with this activity will appear here.​If the pass is not appearing, go to the Passes section > Edit the pass by clicking the pencil > Scroll down and you will see the following:​You can restrict a pass by Activity, Category, and/or Establishment. It’s important to understand that these filters work with an “AND” logic, not “OR”.For example, if a class has the category Hatha Yoga and the activity is “Yoga with Lydia”, selecting the activity “Yoga with Lydia” and the category “Yoga” will make the class incompatible with the pass. This is because the category must match exactly. Hatha Yoga is not the same as Yoga.​As a best practice, we recommend setting compatibility using either Activity or Category, rather than both, unless it clearly aligns with your business needs.
Check the compatibility of the pass:You can do this through Classes > Group Activities > Select the activity. You will then see the section for "Compatible Passes" at the top. All the passes that are compatible with this activity will appear here.​
Check the compatibility of the pass:
If the pass is not appearing, go to the Passes section > Edit the pass by clicking the pencil > Scroll down and you will see the following:​
You can restrict a pass by Activity, Category, and/or Establishment. It’s important to understand that these filters work with an “AND” logic, not “OR”.
Activity
Category
Establishment
“AND” logic
For example, if a class has the category Hatha Yoga and the activity is “Yoga with Lydia”, selecting the activity “Yoga with Lydia” and the category “Yoga” will make the class incompatible with the pass. This is because the category must match exactly. Hatha Yoga is not the same as Yoga.​
incompatible
As a best practice, we recommend setting compatibility using either Activity or Category, rather than both, unless it clearly aligns with your business needs.
either Activity or Category
By addressing these factors, you can prevent duplicate purchases and ensure credits appear on the correct profile.

## Viewing and Booking with Available Credits

## Why does a client with a multi-class pass show no credits available when trying to book?

If a pass is configured to be valid from the first booking, you can check this by going to Passes > Edit and clicking the pencil icon. Verify the pass’s validity settings. For example, if the pass is set to start from the first booking and the member has already scheduled their first booking for next week, the pass will be valid starting next week. In this case, the member cannot use the pass this week, since its validity begins with their initial booking, which is already scheduled.​
Passes > Edit
- Cancel the existing first-dated booking.
Cancel the existing first-dated booking.
- Book the earlier session first, making it the new start date for the pass validity.
Book the earlier session first, making it the new start date for the pass validity.
- Re-add the later booking.
Re-add the later booking.
This adjustment ensures the pass validity aligns with the client’s booking needs.

## Why did past bookings appear to consume valid passes after a membership pause ended?

When a membership pause is applied, bookings can still be created during the pause period. Once the membership resumes, the system allocates valid passes to those previously created bookings. This behaviour is expected and ensures that bookings made during the pause are accounted for once the membership is active again.

## Conclusion

By understanding and configuring passes effectively, you can resolve common issues related to credit discrepancies, compatibility, and booking. Use the steps outlined above to troubleshoot and optimise pass management in bsport.

### How to Manage Unexpected Subscription Charges and Billing Settings in bsport
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/14432044-how-to-manage-unexpected-subscription-charges-and-billing-settings-in-bsport

## How to Manage Unexpected Subscription Charges and Billing Settings in bsport

Managing subscription charges and billing settings effectively is crucial to avoid unexpected fees and ensure smooth operations. This article addresses common issues related to subscription charges, duplicate payments, and billing date adjustments in bsport, along with actionable solutions.

## Understanding Subscription Charges

### Why was I charged before my subscription started?

In some cases, you may notice charges before your subscription officially begins. This typically happens when transaction fees from your clients’ online payments are included in an earlier invoice. For example, a February invoice might reflect only transaction fees (e.g., payment processing fees) and not the subscription itself. ​In Settings > bsport subscription, you can download the invoice of your payment to bsport under "My Invoices". On this invoice you can see the breakdown of your subscription payment. This will include a field "Payment Fees". These are your Stripe transaction fees for the previous month. You can further review the breakdown of these fees in Reporting > Payments report to confirm the details.
transaction fees
Settings > bsport subscription
My Invoices
Reporting > Payments

### Why am I still being charged if I thought my contract should have ended?

For bsport subscriptions, the 12-month term starts from the date of the first successful payment, not from the signature date. Here’s how it works:
- The contract is signed.
The contract is signed.
- If a free trial period applies, billing starts after that period.
If a free trial period applies, billing starts after that period.
- The first payment date sets the start of the 12-month term.
The first payment date sets the start of the 12-month term.
- The term ends after 12 paid months, and the plan renews annually unless canceled in advance.
The term ends after 12 paid months, and the plan renews annually unless canceled in advance.
You can further review this on your contract under the heading "VALIDITY AND DURATION". To avoid unwanted renewals, ensure you submit a cancellation request several weeks before the term ends, as specified in your agreement.
as specified in your agreement

## Managing Billing Dates and SEPA Transfers

### Why was a member charged before the 1st of the month via SEPA?

This happens when the setting to send SEPA transfer orders in advance is enabled. With this option, SEPA payments are initiated a few days earlier to account for banking network delays, so members may see charges dated before the 1st even if the invoice shows the 1st. To ensure charges occur on the 1st of the month:
- Navigate to Settings > Billing.
Navigate to Settings > Billing.
Settings > Billing
- Disable the option to send SEPA transfer orders in advance.
Disable the option to send SEPA transfer orders in advance.
Note: Disabling this option ensures SEPA payments are initiated on the billing date (e.g., the 1st), but enabling it can help account for banking delays.

## General Tips for Subscription Management

- Regularly review your subscription settings and billing reports to identify any discrepancies early.
Regularly review your subscription settings and billing reports to identify any discrepancies early.
- Communicate billing policies clearly to your clients to avoid confusion.
Communicate billing policies clearly to your clients to avoid confusion.
- Submit cancellation requests well in advance to prevent unwanted renewals.
Submit cancellation requests well in advance to prevent unwanted renewals.
By following these guidelines, you can effectively manage subscription charges and billing settings in bsport, ensuring a seamless experience for both you and your clients.

Once you have created your group activities/workshops and added the sessions, they will appear in the calendar.
Sessions display in the calendar, for each day, according to their starting time.
according to their starting time.
However, if several sessions start at the same time, the sessions scheduled at the same hour will display chronologically according to their order of creation.
several sessions start at the same time
order of creation.
This means the last added session will display at the bottom, and the first added session will display at the top.
Example :
Example :
Here, 4 sessions are scheduled on the 17th at 12:00 PM.
The display order above means the Pilates session has been created first, and the MMA class session is the one that has been added the most recently.
If you want to change the display order of the sessions that start at the same time, you need to delete and create again the sessions that you want to see displayed at the bottom.
change the display order
delete and create again

This feature will be useful if one of your teachers is absent for several sessions and you wish to appoint a substitute for all of these sessions.

Step 1:

Step 2: Indicate the substitute teacher
Step 2:

You can indicate in the box the person who can replace the usual teacher.
Step 3: Apply the substitution to the next similar sessions
Step 3:
If the selected session is a recurrent course, it will be possible to propagate this modification to the following sessions. To do this, activate the option below:

You can personalize the name and/or description of this particular session if you want to showcase a special event such as an anniversary, a themed class etc.. ### Intercom
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/7969371-create-a-hybrid-session

You can create hybrid classes that are both on-site and online. This option is available for both group activities and workshops.
Step 1: Create the sessions
Step 1: Create the sessions
Complete the session creation form with the parameters you want for the on-site session.
Activate the toggle named "Hybrid session".
This will create 2 sessions at once: one will be on-site, the other online.
It also works when creating recurring sessions, creating duplicate sessions for all recurrences.
Note: Hybrid sessions are only available for activities and workshops that have been indicated as not Livestream. Check on your activities and workshops that the "Livestream" checkbox is unchecked.
Note:
Step 2: Edit the online session
Step 2: Edit the online session
The online session is automatically named “{Session Name} Online” and has by default 100 available spots.
You can edit the number of available slots, the size of the waiting list, and the number of credits required for the online session.
However, since the online session is linked to the on-site session, other settings such as time and date and teacher can only be modified by editing the on-site session.
Step 3: Manage your sessions
Step 3: Manage your sessions
Hybrid sessions are easily recognizable on the Backoffice Calendar thanks to their specific icon, as well as the helper text that appears when hovering over them.

### Step 1 - Create tags
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/6149203-how-to-add-tags-to-your-sessions

By adding tags to your sessions you can make them invisible and not bookable for members who don't have the right tags. This feature allows you for example to hide advanced sessions from novice members.​

## Step 1 - Create tags

Step 1 - Create tags
Create your tags from the "Marketing" tab > "Tags". Click here for instructions.

## Step 2 - Add tags to sessions from the calendar

Step 2 - Add tags to sessions from the calendar
Go to the "Calendar" tab > Click on the session in question > Click on "Edit".
In the "Advanced" tab at the bottom, select the allowed and not allowed tags.

## Step 3 - Display or not the sessions to which the member does not have access with the tags​

Step 3 - Display or not the sessions to which the member does not have access with the tags
By default, members who don't have the right tags will not see the tagged sessions on their calendar. You can choose to have them appear on the calendar anyway, the member will not be able to book them.​
Go to the "Settings" tab > Click on "Personalisation" > Check "Do not display sessions that the member does not have access to with tags".
Settings
Personalisation
Do not display sessions that the member does not have access to with tags
If you wish to apply tag restrictions to your appointments, see our related article on How to add tags to your appointments

## Page overview and main actions

The Schedule page is your studio-wide calendar for managing studio resources and activities in real time.
You can use it to:
- Display both activities and appointments in daily, weekly or monthly view
Display both activities and appointments in daily, weekly or monthly view
Display both activities and appointments
- Manage teacher and establishment availability
Manage teacher and establishment availability
Manage teacher and establishment availability
- View who teaches which class and where
View who teaches which class and where
View who teaches which class and where
- Filter by teacher, establishment or activity
Filter by teacher, establishment or activity
- Identify scheduling conflicts
Identify scheduling conflicts
Identify scheduling conflicts
- Create recurring sessions
Create recurring sessions
Create recurring sessions
- View bookings and unpaid appointments
View bookings and unpaid appointments
View bookings and unpaid appointments
In short, the Schedule page is where you:
- Plan availabilities
Plan availabilities
Plan
- Visualize your full activity schedule
Visualize your full activity schedule
Visualize
- Control your resources
Control your resources
Control
- Optimize time management for teachers and establishments
Optimize time management for teachers and establishments
Optimize
The Schedule page contains several elements that make navigation and scheduling easy:
Element
Description
Date Picker
Date Picker
Lets you select the week to display. Click on a day of the week to display it and use the arrows to navigate between weeks.
Today button
Today button
Instantly brings you back to the current date in the calendar.
Zoom In / Zoom Out
Zoom In / Zoom Out
Use the + and – buttons to adjust the calendar’s vertical zoom level. Zooming in shows more detail by hour; zooming out provides an overview of the whole day or week.
View mode (Day / Week / Month)
View mode (Day / Week / Month)
Switch between different calendar views depending on your needs. The Week view is the most commonly used for managing operations.
Week view
Group by
Group by
Lets you organize the calendar by teacher, establishment, or activity. This is helpful when comparing resource availability side by side.
teacher
establishment
activity
Filters
Filters
Allow you to display or hide teacher and establishment availability and to filter focus on specific activity

## Understanding the color codes

The calendar uses several colors and patterns to help you identify information at a glance:
Yellow highlight indicates the current day:
Yellow highlight
Solid colors represent available time slots where the teacher, establishment or activity is free to book.
Solid colors
💡 Please note that availabilities added for specific establishments only will appear with a dark left border:
with a dark left border:
💡 Please note: Teacher and establishment colors are set to green by default. To customize these colors:
Please note:
- Open the Appointment tab from the left menu
Open the Appointment tab from the left menu
Appointment
- Select any appointment
Select any appointment
- Switch to the Calendar view
Switch to the Calendar view
Calendar
- Click on a teacher or establishment name in the filter section
Click on a teacher or establishment name in the filter section
- Choose your desired color from the picker
Choose your desired color from the picker
Red dot appears on activities that have been booked but not yet paid.
Red dot
Striped patterns show when a teacher or establishment is unavailable due to an existing session or appointment.
Striped patterns
💡 Please note: Striped blocks can appear longer than the activity itself because they include configured Availability buffer times. For example, an appointment with 20-minute buffers will display the teacher as unavailable (striped) from 20 minutes before until 20 minutes after the scheduled time.
Please
note:
Clicking on an activity opens its details (number of participants and linked resources):

## Adding an availability

What are availabilities for? By adding availabilities, you make it possible for members to book appointments during the time slots that suit them best, according to teacher and establishment availability.
What are availabilities for?
To add a new availability:
- On the Schedule page, click and drag your cursor across the desired time slot.

## Access-Control (9 articles)

### Manual Entry
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/12620345-understanding-access-control

Access Control refers to the different tools available on bsport that allow you to manage who can access your studio. The overall logic of Access Control is the same across all tools; however, there are two ways for your members to gain access:

## Manual Entry

Manual entry is designed for studios without automatic doors but with staff at the reception desk to welcome members. The staff can use a barcode scanner to retrieve member information and decide whether to grant access.

## Auto Entry

Auto entry is designed for studios equipped with automatic doors and typically operating without staff at the reception. The process is fully automated: members simply click to open the door, and the system determines whether they are allowed to enter.

## Let's get started

Let's see how to set up Access Control based on the type of entry you've selected.
- Activate manual entry
Activate manual entry
- Activate auto entry
Activate auto entry

---

### Scanner configuration
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/9286023-how-to-activate-access-control-manual-entry

Before reading this article, make sure you've read this one first.
Manual entry allows you to check member access validity in real-time and ensure that only authorized members enter your studio. Most important member information is quickly displayed your studio’s receptionists to ensure a seamless flow, both for your members and your staff.

## Scanner configuration

## Step 1 - Order the scanner

Before monitoring the visits to your studio, you will have to order the scanner(s) that will be used to scan members' barcodes on their app
Note that the scanners are not included in the feature and their cost has to be covered by the studio
Here are the scanner references that we recommend: Honeywell Genesis 7580g or Newland FM3056.
Honeywell Genesis 7580g
Newland FM3056.
We recommend these scanners as we were able to test them and make sure the feature is working properly. However, they are not the only scanners on the market that are compatible.
If you wish to use another reference, make sure to meet the following requirements :
- Be able to read barcodes on screens
Be able to read barcodes on screens
- Be able to be plugged into the reception PC via a USB cable
Be able to be plugged into the reception PC via a USB cable
- The configuration of the scanner must allow a high-speed reading mode (otherwise the scanning will not trigger a visit but only the display of a text)
The configuration of the scanner must allow a high-speed reading mode (otherwise the scanning will not trigger a visit but only the display of a text)
- The configuration of the scanner must be able to allow the display of the following format after reading a barcode: "A+number" (for example A629658220923)
The configuration of the scanner must be able to allow the display of the following format after reading a barcode: "A+number" (for example A629658220923)
Please note that should you go for another reference than the recommended one, we can not guarantee that the feature will work properly

## Step 2 - Configure the scanner

Follow the steps of the following documentation to configure your chosen barcode reader: Honeywell barcode scanner configuration or Newland barcode scanner configuration and check that it results in the display of the correct format

## Staff configuration

Once the feature is available on your back-office, go to the Staff page in Settings

## Step 1 - Create a receptionist role

If you do not have a role dedicated to the reception yet, click on "Add role" and name it. If you already have such a role, click on the Edit icon.
For the permissions, give at least the permission to "Perform access monitoring" in Navigation menu > Access Monitoring.
permission to "Perform access monitoring"
We also recommend permitting the "Access location visits history" to facilitate the receptionist tasks.
Access location visits history
For the other permissions, please refer to our helpsheet on the permission details and select what better corresponds to the actions that you want to allow your receptionists to do.

## Step 2 - Create a receptionist staff account

Each receptionist staff account will be able to monitor visits from a fixed place where a scanner and reception desk will be posted. You can create as many receptionist staff accounts as you have areas to monitor entry for.
​For example, you may wish to create a receptionist staff account for the general entry of one of your studios, another one for the entry of a specific sub-part of the studio, and another one for the general entry of another studio.
To do so, click on "Add staff", name it, and enter the generic email and password that will be used when logging in to the computer placed at the entrance of the area.
Then select the "receptionist" role
In the Teacher restriction section, if wanted, apply restrictions on some of the teachers (for more information see our helpsheet on How to restrict the permissions granted to your staff accounts.)
In the Access Monitoring section, indicate here the area for which this access will monitor visits.
- If the desk is at the general entrance of the studio, select the location/address of the studio and leave the establishment field empty (if empty, all establishments are considered selected by default)
If the desk is at the general entrance of the studio, select the location/address of the studio and leave the establishment field empty (if empty, all establishments are considered selected by default)
- If the desk is at the entrance of a specific area of the studio, select the location/address of the studio and the establishment(s) that belong to this area
If the desk is at the entrance of a specific area of the studio, select the location/address of the studio and the establishment(s) that belong to this area
Then click on Save. The receptionist staff account will be added to the list of staff accounts.
You can repeat this step as many times as you need receptionist access.
If you already have a receptionist staff account or if you want to edit the selection, click on the Advanced Settings button and update the Access monitoring selection.
Note that a receptionist staff account without at least a location/address selected will not be able to monitor entries.

## Learn how to set up Access Control

### Connect bsport to Kisi
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/11906806-how-to-activate-access-control-auto-entry

Before reading this article, make sure you've read this one first
To allow auto entry, bsport is partnering with Kisi. Before going further in this article, it is mandatory to get in contact with Kisi so you are fully onboarded and your Kisi account is set up with all your doors configured and correctly linked to hardware. Once this is done, you can go on with this article.

## Connect bsport to Kisi

## Create a group on Kisi

First you will need to create a Group in Kisi. We will use this group as the access rules for your members.
You'll be able to define the doors to which your members will have access to (if you have multiple doors). But also during which time period of the day your members can have access to the doors.
- In Kisi, go to Groups and then Add Group
In Kisi, go to Groups and then Add Group
- Fill the name and description. We recommend indicating clearly in the name that this group is for the bsport users.
Fill the name and description. We recommend indicating clearly in the name that this group is for the bsport users.
- We recommend NOT to toggle Scope to a Place. This allows for more flexibility in the future as this is a setting that cannot be changed once the group is created.
We recommend NOT to toggle Scope to a Place. This allows for more flexibility in the future as this is a setting that cannot be changed once the group is created.
- Click on Add
Click on Add
Once your group is created you should:
- indicate to which door this group has access to
indicate to which door this group has access to
- set up the access schedule to make sure your members won't be able to enter when the studio is not opened
set up the access schedule to make sure your members won't be able to enter when the studio is not opened
You can do so by following Kisi helpsheets here.

## Create a user

We now want to create the user bsport is going to use to unlock the door. We will assign to this user the group create previously, so that members can only access your studio at the right time.
- On Kisi go to Users and click on Add Users
On Kisi go to Users and click on Add Users
- Indicate an email address
Indicate an email address
You need to be able to access this email address as your are going to receive an email on it
- Indicate a name. We recommend bsport
Indicate a name. We recommend bsport
- Then click on Share Access
Then click on Share Access
- In the modale, select the group we just created
In the modale, select the group we just created
- In Role, select Group Manager
In Role, select Group Manager
- Make sure the Notify the User toggle is activated
Make sure the Notify the User toggle is activated
- Click on Share
Click on Share
- And then Add
And then Add
Your user just received an email, open it and follow the steps to create a password. Then connect to this account.

## Get the API Key

To connect bsport to Kisi, you will need to provide the Kisi API key of the user we just created to your Account Manager. Here is how to find your API key:
- Make sure your are connected to the user you just created
Make sure your are connected to the user you just created
- In the top right corner, click on your email and select "My Account"
In the top right corner, click on your email and select "My Account"
- In "My Account", select "API" from the menu on the left and click "Add API key"
In "My Account", select "API" from the menu on the left and click "Add API key"
- In "Name", indicate "bsport"
In "Name", indicate "bsport"
- In "Password", indicate the account password
In "Password", indicate the account password
- Copy the API key and send it to your Account Manager
Copy the API key and send it to your Account Manager
- Wait for your Account Manager to confirm that the link between bsport and Kisi has been established.
Wait for your Account Manager to confirm that the link between bsport and Kisi has been established.

## If you are a franchise

If you are a franchise or a brand using the bsport Master Account. And if you have multiple studios in your Master Account using Access Control auto entry, please repeat the previous steps for each of your franchisees/studios that need Access Control auto entry.
- Create one group for each franchiseesIndicate in the name which franchisee this group controlsIn the groups make sure you select only the doors belonging to the given franchisee
Create one group for each franchisees
- Indicate in the name which franchisee this group controls
Indicate in the name which franchisee this group controls
- In the groups make sure you select only the doors belonging to the given franchisee
In the groups make sure you select only the doors belonging to the given franchisee
- Create one user for each franchisees and assign it the right group
Create one user for each franchisees and assign it the right group
- Generate an API Key for each usersMake sure to indicate clearly to your Account Manager which API Key belongs to which franchisee
Generate an API Key for each users
- Make sure to indicate clearly to your Account Manager which API Key belongs to which franchisee
Make sure to indicate clearly to your Account Manager which API Key belongs to which franchisee

## Choose your security method

Auto entry is based on a click-to-open logic. Meaning that your members can open doors through their app by clicking on a button. Learn how this works by following this helpsheet.
In order to prevent fraud, you want to make sure that your members are actually close to the door when opening it. To reduce this risk, we have different ways to protect your studio:
- geofencing
geofencing
- proximity proof
proximity proof
Please select one of the following security protections and reach out to your account manager for them to activate it.
Whatever method is selected, members always have to be connected to the internet if they want to be able to open doors.

## Geofencing

Geofencing defines a radius around your doors in which a member has to be in order to be able to unlock said doors.
To activate it, please contact your Account Manager and give them the radius (in meters) within which members should be in order to unlock doors.
We recommend setting a radius of at least 500 meters around your entrance. This allows for normal variations in phone localization accuracy due to GPS and network conditions.
Wait for your Account Manager to confirm that everything is in place.
⚠️ If you have enabled geofencing on bsport, make sure the "Geofence restriction" toggle is disabled in your Kisi interface. bsport runs its own geofencing layer: having both active at the same time will prevent access from working entirely.
"Geofence restriction"
disabled

## Proximity proof

Proximity proof uses Bluetooth to make sure that the member’s phone is next to a reader. If it’s the case, the member will be able to open the door.
If they are too far from the reader, the door won’t open. In this scenario, the members still have to click to open the door inside the app.
For proximity proof to work, you need to have a reader for each of your doors. You can order your readers here. We advise the Reader Pro 2.1 (High frequency). ⚠️ Only Kisi reader is compatible with proximity proof. You cannot use another reader.
Only Kisi reader is compatible with proximity proof. You cannot use another reader.
To activate it, please contact your Account Manager.
If you chose this option, please follow the installation guide for proximity proof here.

### Set up your passes and subscriptions
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/12620650-set-up-access-control

This article applies to both auto entry and manual entry. You can check the definition here.
Now that Access Control is enabled on your studio, let's see how to set up your passes and bookings to allow your members to enter.

## Set up your passes and subscriptions

## Passes

To allow your members to enter your studio, they will need to own a pass or subscription giving them access in order to do so:
- In the pass creation flow or edition flow, go to the "Door Access" section
In the pass creation flow or edition flow, go to the "Door Access" section
- Activate the toggle "Grant studio access"
Activate the toggle "Grant studio access"
For auto entry only, once activated, this pass will allow all the members owning it to open every door in your studio. This will also apply to passes already owned by members before the toggle was activated.
open every door
The rights to access the studio will remain valid as long as the pass has not expired.
If this pass is used in a subscription, this subscription will also give access to the studio to the members subscribed. For a pass used in subscriptions, we advise you to indicate "Valid from the billing date" as the start date of the pass.

### Valid from first use

If you granted studio access to the pass, you'll see a new option in the start date options for your pass: Valid from the first use.
This option can be used for day passes for example. To learn how to set up this kind of pass, please follow this helpsheet.

### Off peak passes

On passes, you can restrict booking to certain times. But this option is also compatible with Access Control. This way you can indicate if the pass gives access to your studio on a specific day on specific hours.

### Establishment access

For manual entry you can define if a pass gives access to certain establishments using the restrictions on establishments. By default a pass will give access to all establishments.

## Appointment passes

Appointment passes also have the same toggle to grant access. However, they do not have the valid on first use option or off peak compatibility.

### Establishment access

This feature only applies to manual entry.
Just like for passes, you can define to which establishments the appointment passes can give access to.
To do se we will use the "Compatible appointments" section. If the compatible appointments have a given given establishment, the appointment pass will give access to said establishment.

## Entry based on bookings

If you have appointments, group classes or workshops in your studio and you wish that your members could enter if they have an upcoming session (even without a pass, giving them full access), you can activate this feature.
Go to Access > Settings
By default, the timeframe for entry is less than 59 minutes before the session starts and less than 15 minutes after it has started.
You can edit it if needed.
Then enter the new timeframe to be taken into account and click on Save
Note that this setting is shared by all sessions and all locations.
If you wish not to activate this feature, please reach out to your account manager.
not to activate
reach out to your account manager.

## Access Control logic

Everything is set up for your studio, now let's understand the logic of Access Control and how it decides who can enter or not. Let's continue on this article.

---

### The member can enter
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/11928132-understand-the-logic-of-access-control-and-monitoring-entries

The following logic applies for both manual entry and auto entry, please remember that:
Manual entry allows you to check member access validity in real-time and ensure that only authorized members enter your studio. Most important member information is quickly displayed to your studio’s receptionists to ensure a seamless flow, both for your members and your staff.
Manual entry
Auto entry allows your member to enter autonomously in your studio. This feature removes the necessity of manually controlling each entrance and enables your staff to focus on what is truly important.
Auto entry
Since everything happens under the hood, it is important for you to understand the logic of Access Control, to know when a member can enter and when they cannot.
Before reading this article, make sure you've set up everything correctly for Access Control to work.
- Set up for manual entry
Set up for manual entry
- Set up for auto entry
Set up for auto entry
- Common set up
Common set up
See how your members can find there access credentials here.

## The member can enter

For a member to be able to enter the studio we will check the following:

## 1 - Localisation

To make sure the member is at the right place, the system will check the localisation of the member. For manual entry and auto entry the method used is not the same:

### Manual entry

Localisation will be based on the establishment linked to the staff connected. Only members with passes or bookings compatible with the staff's establishment will be able to enter.

### Auto entry

Localisation will be based on the geolocation of the member's phone. Members will be able to enter only establishments with which their passes or bookings are compatible.
This only applies to passes and group classes. Not appointments or appointment passes.

## 2 - Checking on the passes and subscriptions

Then we will check if the member owns at least one pass with Grant studio access activated.
- If not, we'll move on to part number 3.
If not, we'll move on to part number 3.
- If yes, we'll move on to the other checks of this section.
If yes, we'll move on to the other checks of this section.
Then we'll check if the pass is valid and active:
- The check-in date must be inside if the pass validity period.
The check-in date must be inside if the pass validity period.
- The pass shouldn't be blocked.We strongly advise you to activate the feature Deactivate subscriptions when payments fail in order to block passes linked to subscriptions when a subscription is unpaid.You can activate it in Settings> Billing.
The pass shouldn't be blocked.
- We strongly advise you to activate the feature Deactivate subscriptions when payments fail in order to block passes linked to subscriptions when a subscription is unpaid.
We strongly advise you to activate the feature Deactivate subscriptions when payments fail in order to block passes linked to subscriptions when a subscription is unpaid.
- You can activate it in Settings> Billing.
You can activate it in Settings> Billing.
- The pass shouldn't be reverted (linked to a cancelled invoice).
The pass shouldn't be reverted (linked to a cancelled invoice).
- If the pass is part of a subscription, the subscription should not be currently paused.
If the pass is part of a subscription, the subscription should not be currently paused.
- If the pass is restricted to certain times, the entry should be in the given day and hours.
If the pass is restricted to certain times, the entry should be in the given day and hours.

## 3 - Checking on upcoming appointments

This feature has to be activated, see how.
If this feature is activated and the member did not validate the checks of section number 2, we will check if the member has an upcoming booking and this session is coming up in the indicated buffer time, then access will be granted

## Valid status

If the member validates all the conditions above, they will get the green status:
- For manual entry: it means the member can enter
For manual entry: it means the member can enter
- For auto entry: it means the door will open
For auto entry: it means the door will open
If the member has a booking planned before the end of the day, you will be able to see it in the Next Booking section. You can expand this section to see the name of the session's teacher and the number of the member's spot if applicable.
From here you can also see which pass the member is using and if necessary, you can open the member profile, or bill the member with a new item.

## Warning status

In this case the member as validated all the previous checks to enter, however there are some warnings to be aware of:
- the member has a session planned in an establishment not compatible with the zone that you are monitoring access for (manual entry only)
the member has a session planned in an establishment not compatible with the zone that you are monitoring access for (manual entry only)
- the member uses a pass not compatible with the zone that you are monitoring access for (manual entry only)
the member uses a pass not compatible with the zone that you are monitoring access for (manual entry only)
- the member has a session in a compatible establishment but has an unpaid invoice or a negative account balance
the member has a session in a compatible establishment but has an unpaid invoice or a negative account balance
- the member has a session in a compatible establishment but does not have a pass
the member has a session in a compatible establishment but does not have a pass
- the member has recently changed their profile picture
the member has recently changed their profile picture
The behavior is not going to be the same if it's for manual entry or auto entry:
- Manual entry: An action is required on your part to regularize the member's status and address the warnings before letting the member enter or not.
Manual entry: An action is required on your part to regularize the member's status and address the warnings before letting the member enter or not.
Manual entry:
- Auto entry: The door will still open for the member. However, in the entry history you will see the warning status on the member.
Auto entry: The door will still open for the member. However, in the entry history you will see the warning status on the member.
Auto entry:

## Acknowledge and address warnings

This part is only meant for manual entry
All warnings will appear below the member information and can be addressed from here.
The warning regarding a recent change in the profile picture is the only exception and is displayed next to the member picture
To address this warning, please make sure that the member checking in is the same person as shown on the picture.
To do so, click on "See previous" and open the picture history. If you can confirm that the member matches their picture, click on "Validate identity". The warning will then disappear.
Once you have acknowledged all warnings and if you have addressed some or all of them, you can click on the button "Refresh status".
If all warnings were addressed and the status of the member has changed, you will be notified in the member information

## Indicate if the member is allowed to enter or not

If the member has a warning status, you will have to indicate if the member is allowed to enter or not.
You can decide to allow a member to enter even if not all warnings were addressed, depending on the type of warning and your studio's policy.
To do so, either click on "Refuse entry" or "Allow entry"
From here you can choose to close or to stay on the member page if you want to continue seeing the member information and warnings.
For auto entry, "Allow entry" will be selected automatically.

## Not valid status

This status means that the member doesn't have the right to enter.
Actions are required on your part and the member is not allowed to enter as long as warnings are not addressed.
What is expected from you is not the same if it's for manual entry or auto entry:
- Manual entry: Check on why the member cannot enter and try to solve the situation.
Manual entry: Check on why the member cannot enter and try to solve the situation.
Manual entry:
- Auto entry: The door will not open to the member.
Auto entry: The door will not open to the member.
Auto entry:
From here you can address the warnings and refresh the member status as explained above, or communicate to the member that they cannot enter

## Access history of visits

To see the history of all member's visits that day, go to Access and click on the Location Visits tab
If you are a receptionist of the studio, you will see all visits of members who checked in at your desk (only for manual entries, for auto entries you will see all entries).
If you are an administrator or owner of the studio, you will see the visits of all members who checked in at one of the studio's receptions.
The visit reason will indicate the name of the session the member came for. If there is no session, then it indicate "None".
From here you can open again the member's information by clicking on the button on the right
You will be then able to perform the same actions as mentioned above or go back to the visit history
Note that at the end of the day, the history will be refreshed and emptied. To have a full list of members' visits to your studio on a certain period, you can consult the report Access Monitoring in Reporting.

### Set up the validity period
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/12621911-access-control-passes-activated-on-first-use

If you granted studio access to the pass, you'll see a new option in the start date options for your pass: Valid from the first use.
This option is meant to create "Drop-in passes". Those passes can be used to give one-day access to members who are not subscribed, for example.
"Valid from the first use" passes become active when a member checks in for the first time (opens a door to access the studio or scans their barcode) or does a first booking. Then the pass will be valid for the given number of days (a day, a week ...).

## Set up the validity period

- In the validity section select "Valid from the 1st use (booking or check-in)
In the validity section select "Valid from the 1st use (booking or check-in)
- Then, define the "Activation period". The activation period is the time during which the pass is not activated yet; it's waiting for either a first check-in or booking. If the member doesn't do any of these actions during the activation period, then the pass will expire.
Then, define the "Activation period".
- The activation period is the time during which the pass is not activated yet; it's waiting for either a first check-in or booking. If the member doesn't do any of these actions during the activation period, then the pass will expire.
The activation period is the time during which the pass is not activated yet; it's waiting for either a first check-in or booking. If the member doesn't do any of these actions during the activation period, then the pass will expire.
- Then define the validity period of your pass. Once activated, your pass will become valid for the duration indicated. Your members will be able to access the studio only during the given period.
Then define the validity period of your pass.
- Once activated, your pass will become valid for the duration indicated. Your members will be able to access the studio only during the given period.
Once activated, your pass will become valid for the duration indicated. Your members will be able to access the studio only during the given period.

## Activation

This pass will be activated with the following actions:
- A first bookingThe pass validity will start on the date of the booked session
A first booking
- The pass validity will start on the date of the booked session
The pass validity will start on the date of the booked session
- A first check-inThe member accessed the studios using this pass (opened a door or scanned their bar code)
A first check-in
- The member accessed the studios using this pass (opened a door or scanned their bar code)
The member accessed the studios using this pass (opened a door or scanned their bar code)

## First booking edge case

- If the first booking of this pass is canceled and there is no other bookings done with the pass:The pass validity will fall back to the activation period and will be waiting for another booking or first check-in.
If the first booking of this pass is canceled and there is no other bookings done with the pass:
- The pass validity will fall back to the activation period and will be waiting for another booking or first check-in.
The pass validity will fall back to the activation period and will be waiting for another booking or first check-in.
- If the first booking of this pass is canceled and there is another booking:The pass validity will start on the date of the next closest booking in time.
If the first booking of this pass is canceled and there is another booking:
- The pass validity will start on the date of the next closest booking in time.
The pass validity will start on the date of the next closest booking in time.

## Manual activation

Passes that activate on first use can be activated manually by going on the member profile in the "passes" tab.
Clicking on the "Activate" button, activates the validity of the pass.
This button appears only if:
- the pass is activable on first use
the pass is activable on first use
- there has been no first check-in
there has been no first check-in
- the pass is not expired
the pass is not expired
Note the button still appears even though a first booking has been done. The goal is to allow your staff to change the activation date to today if a member comes to the studio and wants to have access before the day of their first booking.
This comes in handy for drop in passes that are valid for a week.
It is important to note that if the new start date of the pass, makes it so that some bookings are not inside of the validity of the pass anymore, the bookings will not be canceled automatically. The staff will be warned and will decide what to do.

---

### Manual entry
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/11928083-how-can-my-members-access-my-studio

Access Control needs to be activated on your account for this feature to appear on your members' app.
Remember that for Access Control, there are 2 was for your members to enter:
- Manual entry
Manual entry
- Auto entry
Auto entry
The flow to enter will be different based on which method is used to enter.

## Manual entry

Note that if you are logged in as an administrator or owner of the studio, you will not be able to scan members, but you will always have access to the live list of members' visits.
The members checking in at the reception desk can now scan their bar-codes visible on their studio app in the Profile tab
Tips to optimize the scanning :
- barcodes are detected more easily by the scanner when they are compact i.e. not too zoomed in on the screen
barcodes are detected more easily by the scanner when they are compact i.e. not too zoomed in on the screen
- if possible, avoid placing your mouse cursor in an active text field in the back-office
if possible, avoid placing your mouse cursor in an active text field in the back-office
Note that the members' bar-codes cannot be scanned if your active tab is not a tab of the back-office (for example any Google page)
Each time a member scans their bar-codes, you will see a notification indicating the member's name and entry status. If you wish to see more information about this member entry, click on "Open".

## Auto entry

Auto entry uses the logic of click-to-open, meaning that your members will have a button in their app in order to be able to unlock your doors.

## Unlock a door

In their app, your members will have to:
- Go to their Profile tab
Go to their Profile tab
- Click on the "Unlock door" button
Click on the "Unlock door" button
- A list of all the doors will appear
A list of all the doors will appear
- Your member will have to select which door to open
Your member will have to select which door to open
- Then click on Unlock
Then click on Unlock
Depending on the logic explained here your member will then be able to enter or not.

## Goefencing and proximity proof

If the Geofencing or proximity proof feature has been activated, your members will have to allow their phone to share their location. If not, the app will ask them again to accept. If they don't, they won't be able to open doors.
It is important that your members activate their exact location (normally activated by default on IOS and Android). If not, the phone will only provide an approximate location, which could prevent members from entering.

## Check on a member status

You can always check on a member's status even if the member didn't try to enter. This can happen if your member doesn't have access to their smartphone or if there are any issues with the hardware.
The following actions can only be done by a staff member with the right permissions. Owners do not have the permissions to do so.

## Searching for a member in the search bar

Searching for a member in the search bar
From any page of the back-office, click on the member search bar on the top right of your screen​
and search for the member's name or email. Then on the list of results, click on "Member entry" next to the member name ​
You will see a notification indicating the member's name and entry status. If you wish to see more information about this member entry, click on "Open"

## Searching for a member in the Member Visit tab

Searching for a member in the Member Visit tab
First, on the navigation menu, click on "Access" to open the Access Monitoring page in a new tab.
Then on the Member visit page, click on the member search bar and search for the member name or email.
On the list of results, click on the right member. The information about this member visit will now appear on the same page.
In the case of auto entry, the manual checks will no open the door. You can do it by going on the Kisi interface.

---

### Plug your reader
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/13350916-how-to-activate-proximity-proof-for-auto-entry

Before reading this article, make sure you read this one first.
Now that your Account Manager has activated the feature and you've received your readers, you'll have to set them up for the feature to work.

## Plug your reader

First, you'll have to plug in your reader and connect it to the internet. To do so, you can follow Kisi's help sheets.
- To install your reader on the wall
To install your reader on the wall
- To wire your reader
To wire your reader

## Our tip

Wiring your reader may not work from the get-go, as most Ethernet wall sockets do not supply power. You need a Power over Ethernet socket. Most of the time, these sockets have 2 LEDs, one for power, the other for the internet.
If your router or wall socket doesn't have this kind of port, then you will need a power injector. Here is an example of one. To note: We didn't test this product ourselves and cannot assure good functioning. Reach out to Kisi if you have any doubts.
To note:

## Set up on Kisi

Once your reader is properly plugged in, you'll have to set up the reader on the Kisi backoffice to connect it to the desired door.
To do so, follow Kisi's help sheet.
Once you are done, in the Kisi backoffice:
- Select the corresponding place
Select the corresponding place
- Click on Doors in the left side menu
Click on Doors in the left side menu
- Select the wanted door
Select the wanted door
- Go to Permissions
Go to Permissions
- Activate Reader Proximity Restriction
Activate Reader Proximity Restriction
- Save
Save
You can also select how close your members should be to the reader to be able to unlock the door. To do so:
- Select the corresponding place
Select the corresponding place
- Go to Hardware
Go to Hardware
- Scroll down to Sensitivity
Scroll down to Sensitivity
- Change it to the wanted value
Change it to the wanted value
- Save
Save

## Our tip

There is no need for your members to tap their phone on the reader. This will actually activate NFC (Apple Wallet, Google Wallet...), which will override the bsport interface.
We recommend that your members simply step in front of the door and unlock it from there.

---

### Controller (must have)
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/13671343-which-hardware-to-order-for-access-control-autro-entry

In order to make sure your Access Control auto-entry installation works as best as possible, it's important to have a clear mind the hardware you need to order from Kisi.

## Controller (must have)

The controller is the only must-have piece of hardware for this integration.
This piece of hardware is going to be wired to your door. It does the link between the door and Kisi. This is how Kisi can control your door and send orders to open the door or stay put.
Without this piece of hardware, we cannot make the link between what happens on the software and what happens in the real world.
To know about the controller:
- Controller is free thanks to bsport and Kisi partnership
Controller is free thanks to bsport and Kisi partnership
free
- You can order it here
You can order it here
You can learn how to install your controller by following Kisi's helpsheet:
- Wire and install your controller
Wire and install your controller

## Reader (optional)

The reader is only necessary if you want to use proximity proof (see).
Thanks to the reader and using Bluetooth, we will know that your members' phone is next to the door when they try to open it.
To know about the reader:
- Reader costs 475$ (one-time payment)
Reader costs 475$ (one-time payment)
- You can order it here
You can order it here
Learn how to install your reader here.

## Other cases

In the case you already have a controller or reader from another provider, and you wish to keep them. You'll have to check with Kisi which hardware is needed to make your previous hardware compatible with the new one.
Anyway, you'll have to order a controller.

## Delivery time and custom fees

Kisi is based in the US. Take into account a 1-month delivery time if you are in Europe. Also, it is important to check the customs fees in your country when it comes to deliveries from the US.

Off-peak passes can be created from studio's back-office and from franchise master account.

Step 2: Configure the time slots for which the pass will be compatible.

On the member's side:

The pass cannot be used outside the specified time slots.
Please note that a session that is partly within the defined time slots and partly outside will be considered as outside the defined time slots, i.e. the pass cannot be used.
For sessions within the time slots, the pass works as usual.

### Intercom
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/6117685-understanding-the-theoretical-marginal-value

When creating a pass with unlimited credits, it is possible to add the theoretical marginal value including VAT.
This tool allows you to estimate the specific value of a booking, and is mainly used to calculate the teacher payroll.
For example, inputting 10€ means that the value of the booking made with this pass is 10€.
If left empty, the marginal contribution is calculated as follows:
Price of the pass /Number of reservations made by the member with that same pass.
Price of the pass /Number of reservations made by the member with that sam

### Intercom
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/6600403-make-membership-registration-fees-mandatory-for-the-purchase-of-a-pass

Step 1: Create a tag
Step 1:
Click on "Marketing" > "Tags" > "Add a main tag".
Once this is done, click on the "+" button.
Then, you have to create two tags: "Yes" and "No".
Step 2: Create a category and a card: membership fee
Step 2
Click on "Products" > "Passes" > "+ add a category".
Then click on "+ add a pass" which we will call "Membership fee".
Then set the number of credits to 0 and in our example, the annual fee will be 10€.
Then choose the validity: in our example it is an annual fee so we will choose 1 year.
And then, you can click on save.
Step 3: Create the smartlist to automatically apply the tag
Step 3:
Click on "Marketing" > "Smartlists" > "+ add a smartlist" and create the smartlist.
Then you have to "add a filter".
Select "Passes" > "Passes"
Select "Owns".
Select the pass "Registration fees".
Click on  "Add".
Then you have to add the automatic tagging rules.
First, the rule "tag if present in the smartlist" for members who ALREADY own the card.
You will have to choose the member tag "yes".
And click on the save button.
Then repeat the operation with the rule: "tag if a member enters in this Smartlist".
NOTE: Automatic tagging can take up to 24 hours to update.
NOTE: Automatic tagging can take up to 24 hours to update.
Step 4: Make your course cards visible ONLY to members.
Step 4:
Click on "Products" > "Passes" > Click on the pencil on the right.
Scroll to the bottom, then click on "Advanced".
Search for the tag "members - yes" that we created.
And then click on "Edit".
Repeat for ALL the passes except membership fees.
NOTE:
NOTE:
- If your registration fees are annual, we recommend that you name the membership card "Membership Fee 2023" for example. The next one will be "Registration fees 2024", the next one "Registration fees 2025" etc...
If your registration fees are annual, we recommend that you name the membership card "Membership Fee 2023" for example. The next one will be "Registration fees 2024", the next one "Registration fees 2025" etc...
- You will have to repeat the steps described above for each new card you create.
You will have to repeat the steps described above for each new card you create.

### Intercom
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/7973720-create-an-early-bird-pass

When creating a pass, an appointment pass or a pack, you can set up a date limit for purchase. This is not the expiration date for the validity of the pass, but a limit after which members can't buy the product. Once the date limit is reached, the product will still exist but will not appear on the Marketplace.
This feature is especially useful to apply an early bird pricing strategy, in which the product is sold at a discount, but the customers have to buy it quickly before the opportunity disappears.

### Intercom
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/7262426-penalties-on-no-show

Sometimes some of your members do not cancel their session and do not come to it. In order to avoid this type of situation, it is important to be able to make your members aware of the need to cancel their session in good time if they do not wish to attend.
In this sheet, you will learn how to apply penalties to your absent members who used an unlimited pass to make their booking.
Note: Penalties are only applicable to unlimited passes.
Note:
Step 1: Apply a penalty on unlimited passes
Step 1:
To apply these penalties on no-shows, you must indicate it on the unlimited passes. So, go to Product > Passes and create or modify an unlimited pass.
You will then be able to apply a penalty:
Select the one concerning no-shows:
You can also select in parallel the penalties for late cancellations as they are independent.
Then define the number of no-shows from which you wish to penalize a member with an unlimited pass:
And finally, choose the penalty you wish to apply to him. You have the choice between :
- Temporarily block the unlimited pass used for booking. In this case, you must indicate the number of days the pass will not be usable
Temporarily block the unlimited pass used for booking. In this case, you must indicate the number of days the pass will not be usable
- Deduct an amount (to be defined) from the customer balance.
Deduct an amount (to be defined) from the customer balance.
You can set different penalty settings depending on the unlimited passes.
Step 2: Triggering the roll call
Step 2:
When you create a penalty on no-shows for the first time: a message tells you that you have unlocked the possibility to "Make the roll call". To find out more, see our help sheet on the subject.
This will allow you to note which member was present/absent during the session and then validate the roll call. Thanks to this validation of the roll call, a member's absences will be taken into account.
Step 3: No-show settings
Step 3:
Finally, go to Settings > Personalization: there are two parameters to be indicated concerning no-shows.
- The time between validation of the roll call and final no-show status
The time between validation of the roll call and final no-show status
The time between validation of the roll call and final no-show status
To give you time to rectify the situation, absences can be modified for a certain period of time after the roll call has been validated.
When a member is indicated as absent during a roll call, choose in these parameters the time (in hours or in days) before their no-show status is definitively indicated.
- The time between the validation of the roll call and the sending of the email to warn of an absence
The time between the validation of the roll call and the sending of the email to warn of an absence
The time between the validation of the roll call and the sending of the email to warn of an absence
If a member is indicated as absent during a session, an email will automatically be sent to indicate this. The purpose of this email is to warn members that they were absents at a session and to give them time to contact you again to modify their status if there was an error.
This will allow them to avoid being marked as permanently absent (no-show) and taking a penalty. From the settings, you can define how long (in hours or days) the email will be sent after the validation of the roll call.
⚠️ To give the member time to react, the email must be sent before the final validation of the absent status.
There you have it: you have everything in place to penalize no-shows of your members using unlimited passes. All you have to do is make the roll call on your sessions!
As a reminder, you can find the roll call help sheet by following this link.

### Intercom
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/4668093-activate-the-penalty-system-on-passes-with-unlimited-credits

1st Step: Modify passes with unlimited credits.
1st Step:
Managing cancellations and no-shows effectively is crucial for maintaining operational efficiency and fairness in your membership policies.
On the platform go to Products > Passes and edit the pass with unlimited credits of your choice.
Select the unlimited pass you want to configure or create a new one if necessary.
Scroll down to the "Penalties" section. Enable the options for "no-shows" and/or "late cancellations" to activate the penalty system.
2nd step: Activate the penalty system.
2nd step:
Tick the option "Too many late cancelations" and fill in the different options available:
Specify the time window (e.g., 30 days) for counting these incidents.
- Number of cancelations after which the penalty will take place
Number of cancelations after which the penalty will take place
- Number of days where the cancellations took place
Number of days where the cancellations took place
- Choose the penalty type: Temporarily block the pass or charge the member's account balance
Choose the penalty type: Temporarily block the pass or charge the member's account balance
For example, you can allow 2 no-shows and 2 late cancellations per month without a fee or set a monetary penalty such as a €10 charge for no-shows.
Click on "Modify" to save.
ATTENTION: the penalty system is only applicable on passes with unlimited credits!
ATTENTION: the penalty system is only applicable on passes with unlimited credits!
This limitation ensures that penalties are applied fairly and do not penalize members with limited credit passes unnecessarily.
