### All reports page
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/9721753-reports-2-0

A new version of the reports has been released! The objective of this new version is to make readability and navigation easier for you on the reports so that you can easily find the exact piece of information you are looking for.
Until the 1st of October, you can switch back and forth between the old and new versions by clicking on the "Back to old version" button. However, changes made on one version will not appear on the other.
will not
After this date, the old version will be permanently out of access.
Check below what is new with version 2.0

## All reports page

On the first page, you will now see a better distribution of the reports' types, still sorted by their categories.
As before, you can search a report type by its name in the search bar to find it more easily

## Report detail page

Once you open a report type, you can navigate between different views. The views are made of :
- One default view, that is always accessible and cannot be deleted. You can however edit the data displayed via the edit view form
One default view, that is always accessible and cannot be deleted. You can however edit the data displayed via the edit view form
One default view
- All your migrated views form the 1.0 version of the reports if you had created any for that report's type. These views were renamed "[Migrated] name" for you to identify them.
All your migrated views form the 1.0 version of the reports if you had created any for that report's type. These views were renamed "[Migrated] name" for you to identify them.
All your migrated views
- All future views, that you will create from the 2.0 version of the reports
All future views, that you will create from the 2.0 version of the reports
All future views
Note that if you had created several versions of a report in the 1.0 version (e.g. several "Basket" reports), they are now all migrated as views in the report type "Basket".Quickfilters, advanced filters, and date filters were all saved as well for every view
If you wish to create a new view of this report, for example to apply specific filters, click on the "Add view" button
Then name the view and click on confirm. The default view and all new views created display all available columns and data and have no quick or advanced filters.

## Advanced filters and general display

If you wish to build a view that always displays only the data that you need, click on the "Edit" icon
From here you can decide to rename the view, apply Advanced filters, or select/unselect the columns you want to appear in the view. Then click on "Save and Generate"
Tip :
Tip
- Advanced filters are made to be permanently applied to your view and to be really specific thanks to and/or rules management. Use these filters if you want your view to be always filtered with these conditions
Advanced filters are made to be permanently applied to your view and to be really specific thanks to and/or rules management. Use these filters if you want your view to be always filtered with these conditions
Advanced filters
- Quick filters are meant to be temporary filters that can be quickly applied and removed from a view. Use these filters if you want to temporarily access a specific piece of information in the view.
Quick filters are meant to be temporary filters that can be quickly applied and removed from a view. Use these filters if you want to temporarily access a specific piece of information in the view.
Quick filters
For more information, consult our helpsheet on filtering the data in the reports

## Date and quick filters

### Date filters

Once the view is built according to your needs, you can filter the data on a certain date/period or items.
Most of the reports can be filtered for a specific period by clicking on the date selector. You can quickly select a period or select "custom" if you have a particular period in mind. You can also click on the date/hour range selector to define your custom perio.
specific period
With version 2.0, new date filters are available, and you can now choose to filter the view to the week, month or year to date, i.e. from the first day of the selected period to the current day.
Some reports can be filtered by a unique date only and allow you to quickly filter on today's date or yesterday or to choose a custom date
Note that a reminder of what the selected period is filtering on is available next to the date/time selector

### Quick filters

Then you can apply quick filters to the view by clicking on "Add a quick filter" and selecting the item you want to filter on
quick filters
Once you have selected the date and quick filters that you possibly need, quick on "Generate report". All data below will then take into account your set up
As with version 1.0, you can download the report in xlsx format. The export will save advanced filters as well as date and quick filters

## Delete a view

To delete a view that you don't need anymore, click on the bin icon next to the view. All views can be deleted except for the default one.

## Compare reports

With version 2.0 of the reports, you will now be able to navigate faster between reports to possibly compare data. You can switch from one report to another by using the navigation menu on the left.
From this navigation menu, you can also go back to the "All reports" page
Here is an explanatory video :
If you have feedback on version 2.0 of the reports, please do not hesitate to report it on the Feedback board accessible from your account.

### Intercom
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/7860910-create-google-tag-manager-tags-to-track-facebook-ad-data-gtm

Once your Facebook Pixels account and Google Tag Manager are linked, you will now be able to create GTM tags, linking them to a Pixel to track activity on Facebook Pixels. To learn how to link both accounts, check this article.
First things first:
First things first:
To allow Pixels to track events that happen on the bsport platform, you need to go through Google Tag Manager. That's why you connected Google Tag Manager and Pixels in the previous step.
You also need to make Google Tag Manager understand the events that happen on the bsport platform so that it can send them to Pixels.
It is necessary to create a variable on Google Tag Manager which corresponds to the bsport event.
Find all the bsport events here: https://pastebin.pl/view/23083ef7
We are now going to create the variable for a bsport event to be sent to Pixels.
For this example we will choose the click on add to cart event.
Let's create the variable. In Google Tag Manager, go to Variables > New.
Then create a new variable > Data Layer Variable.
Put "data" in the name of the data layer variable. Name the variable, for us it will be "dlv - data".
So we have this for the configuration:
We can now move on to configuring the tag.
In the Google Tag Manager platform, you can go to "Add a tag".
Then you can go to "Configure tag" and then to "Discover other types of tags".
Then you have to look for the "Facebook Pixel", and you can easily configure Pixels in GTM this way and not go through the manual code.
Then you have to configure the Pixel by naming it, putting an ID, choosing the event.
We will choose the Add to Cart event.
In the same way, you must configure the triggering of this tag. Go to "Trigger" at the bottom and configure it.
You need to create a trigger, to do this you need to click on the "+" at the top right and create a trigger. Then you have to configure a "Custom Event".
In "Object Properties", you must add the variable created earlier.
So add "dlv-data".
Then you have to configure the triggering of this tag. Go to "Trigger" at the bottom and configure it.
You need to create a trigger, to do this click on the "+" at the top right and create a trigger. Then you have to configure a "Custom Event".
From here we can name the trigger. "Add to Basket" for our example will work fine.
In "Item Name" we need to enter the bsport event we want to pull up. Our item being the add-to-cart, in the file attached higher in this form we know that the bsport event that corresponds to that is: bsport:basket:add-to-cart:pass
bsport:basket:add-to-cart:pass
Don't forget to check the "Use regular expression matching" box.
Don't forget to check the "Use regular expression matching" box.
The configuration is finished! You can save and switch to preview mode in Google Tag Manager. Google Tag Manager's preview or "debug" mode allows you to see the tags that are triggered on a website page. There is also the Chrome extension, Google Tag Assistant.
Go to your bsport marketplace and click on an add to cart of your platform.
In the GTM "Debug" you can see that the tag has been triggered.
You can also check if the Pixel has been activated with the Facebook extension Pixel Helper.
You can also check in the Facebook Pixel Event Manager:
There you go, you can now set up Google Tag Manager events related to your platform and pull them up in Pixels.

#### For more information regarding how to use the Facebook Pixels and Google Tag Manager, please visit this article

### Intercom
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/5370140-facebook-pixels-bsport-google-tag-manager

N.B.: the screenshots in this sheet are from October 2020, the interface and the Facebook Pixels platform change regularly so the appearance may not be the same. Nevertheless, the process should remain the same.
N.B.: the screenshots in this sheet are from October 2020, the interface and the Facebook Pixels platform change regularly so the appearance may not be the same. Nevertheless, the process should remain the same.
First of all, what is Google Tag Manager?
First of all, what is Google Tag Manager?
Google Tag Manager (GTM) is a tag management system that allows you to track more advanced data. GTM quickly updates and facilitates the tracking of data on applications or websites. It is an interface that allows you to create and view the tags you create. GTM is a Tag Management System, and works like a CMS (Content Management System) except that it is not for site content but for special tags.
You can track your data by linking your Google Tag Manager account to Google Analytics for example.
Why do we need Google Tag Manager?
Why do we need Google Tag Manager?
The advantages of Google Tag Manager:
- Saves time for developers. They don't need to develop it in-house, understandable interface so anyone can implement it.
Saves time for developers. They don't need to develop it in-house, understandable interface so anyone can implement it.
- More accurate data. Allows for custom tracking, allows for analysis of specific data/needs.
More accurate data. Allows for custom tracking, allows for analysis of specific data/needs.
- Increases site speed (response time, workflow, page load time)
Increases site speed (response time, workflow, page load time)
Why set it up for your bsport account?
Why set it up for your bsport account?
To be able to analyse the behaviour of students and potential students when they are on the bsport platform linked to your account. You can analyse the different steps, clicks, sessions and others.
What are Facebook Pixels?
What are Facebook Pixels?
The Facebook Pixel is a piece of code that allows you to retrieve data from users who have visited your website in order to build an audience.
Why do we need Facebook Pixels?
Why do we need Facebook Pixels?
The Facebook Pixel is used to optimise advertising campaigns on Instagram and Facebook, to analyse traffic to your site via Facebook, and to track purchase path and other data related to advertising.
Why to set it up for its bsport account?
Why to set it up for its bsport account?
To be able to analyse the behaviour of users after clicking on a Facebook or Instagram ad. To have the right data to optimize your marketing campaigns.
Now you know what Google Tag Manager and Facebook Pixels are, let's start this tutorial to link these two tools to your bsport platform.
The different steps to be able to get the bsport data:
- Link your bsport account and Google Tag Manager
Link your bsport account and Google Tag Manager
- Setting up your Google Tag Manager account
Setting up your Google Tag Manager account
- Set up your Facebook Pixels account
Set up your Facebook Pixels account
- Link Pixels and Google Tag Manager
Link Pixels and Google Tag Manager
- Create Google Tag Manager tags to track Facebook ads data
Create Google Tag Manager tags to track Facebook ads data
- Check that actions on the platform are reported to Pixels
Check that actions on the platform are reported to Pixels
For this tutorial you will need:
- A Google Tag Manager account
A Google Tag Manager account
- A Facebook Business Manager account, which gives access to Facebook Pixels
A Facebook Business Manager account, which gives access to Facebook Pixels
- A bsport account
A bsport account
- Link your Google Tag Manager account and bsport
Link your Google Tag Manager account and bsport
To link your Google Tag Manager account and bsport you only need two things:
- To know your Google Tag Manager identifier
To know your Google Tag Manager identifier
- Enter your Google Tag Manager ID in the bsport platform
Enter your Google Tag Manager ID in the bsport platform
To know your Google Tag Manager ID you just have to go to the GTM interface and find your ID.
You can find it here:
The number next to GTM is your ID.
Then, go to your bsport platform as an admin.
You must enter your Google Tag Manager ID in Settings > General > Google Tag ID
Now your bsport account and Google Tag Manager are linked.
Set up your Google Tag Manager account
Set up your Google Tag Manager account
Now you can set up your GTM account to track your platform data.
Google Tag Manager works with 3 settings:
- Tags. Creating one allows you to set rules, to choose what you want to track. This can be a page view, pull information from Google Analytics/Facebook Pixels, etc/
Tags. Creating one allows you to set rules, to choose what you want to track. This can be a page view, pull information from Google Analytics/Facebook Pixels, etc/
Tags
- Triggers, the data you want to track, tag that will be triggered by a certain action. For example: click on video, click on article, add cart, etc.
Triggers, the data you want to track, tag that will be triggered by a certain action. For example: click on video, click on article, add cart, etc.
Triggers
- Variables, variables that can be applied to the triggers to have a more accurate tracking
Variables, variables that can be applied to the triggers to have a more accurate tracking
Variables
You can now create tags in order to track the data on your bsport platform.
To analyse them, you must link your Google Tag Manager account to Google Analytics.
you must link your Google Tag Manager account to Google Analytics.
To create a tag in Google Tag Manager click on 'Tabs' in the left sidebar, then new.
After creating a Google Analytics account and linking it to Google Tag Manager, this is done automatically.
Then on the Google Tag Manager platform, you have to create a tag > Google Analytics: Universal Analytics
The configuration is done automatically and all you have to do is choose the variable and the trigger for the tag.
For the triggering of the tag you have to choose "All Pages" Page View, so that the tag is triggered each time it is viewed/uploaded on the platform.
Once done, you will be taken to a page that looks like this:
You can preview, check that the tag is triggered when viewed on the platform/on your site and publish!
This is the same principle for all other possible tags on Google Tag Manager.
Setting up your Facebook Pixels account
Setting up your Facebook Pixels account
You need to have a Facebook Business Manager account, to manage ads and also to create and moderate Pixels. To create an account: https://business.facebook.com/overview
After creating an account, go to the Event Manager. From there you can create a Pixel.
You can create a Pixel for your site, for the platform, etc.
To do this you need to click on "Add Pixel", then a banner will appear with three possibilities:
- Link your Pixel to your site
Link your Pixel to your site
- Link your Pixel to your application
Link your Pixel to your application
- Link your Pixel Offline
Link your Pixel Offline
Choose the Pixel to your site.
This pop up then appears displaying 3 options:
Choose one of the two options, we recommend the partner integration because it is easier.
You will then have this banner displayed:
Then instructions will be given according to the partner chosen and you can analyse your site's data with a few clicks.
You can also install the pixel code in all <head> of your site.
If you have an agency or webmaster, you can also send with "Send instructions by email".
Linking Pixels and Google Tag Manager
Linking Pixels and Google Tag Manager
You can now link Google Tag Manager and Pixels.
By linking Google Tag Manager and Pixels, you can also link with bsport as the GTM tag was configured in step 1.
To connect Facebook Pixels to Google Tag Manager, it happens on the Facebook side.
Go to the Event Manager to create a Pixel.
As in the previous step, add a Pixel to your site and click on "Use a partner".
Then you select Google Tag Manager.
All you have to do is follow the steps and everything will be set up.
Your Pixel and Google Tag Manager are now connected.
Create Google Tag Manager tags to track Facebook ad data
Create Google Tag Manager tags to track Facebook ad data
We will now create GTM tags, linking them to a Pixel to track activity on Facebook Pixels.
First things first:
First things first:
To allow Pixels to track events that happen on the bsport platform, you need to go through Google Tag Manager. That's why you connected Google Tag Manager and Pixels in the previous step.
You also need to make Google Tag Manager understand the events that happen on the bsport platform so that it can send them to Pixels.
It is necessary to create a variable on Google Tag Manager which corresponds to the bsport event.
Find all the bsport events here.
We are now going to create the variable for a bsport event to be sent to Pixels.
For this example we will choose the click on add to cart event.
Let's create the variable. In Google Tag Manager, go to Variables > New.
Then create a new variable > Data Layer Variable.
Put "data" in the name of the data layer variable. Name the variable, for us it will be "dlv - data".
So we have this for the configuration:
We can now move on to configuring the tag.
In the Google Tag Manager platform, you can go to "Add a tag".
Then you can go to "Configure tag" and then to "Discover other types of tags".
Then you have to look for the "Facebook Pixel", and you can easily configure Pixels in GTM this way and not go through the manual code.
Then you have to configure the Pixel by naming it, putting an ID, choosing the event.
We will choose the Add to Cart event.
In the same way, you must configure the triggering of this tag. Go to "Trigger" at the bottom and configure it.
You need to create a trigger, to do this you need to click on the "+" at the top right and create a trigger. Then you have to configure a "Custom Event".
In "Object Properties", you must add the variable created earlier.
So add "dlv-data".
Then you have to configure the triggering of this tag. Go to "Trigger" at the bottom and configure it.
You need to create a trigger, to do this click on the "+" at the top right and create a trigger. Then you have to configure a "Custom Event".
From here we can name the trigger. "Add to Basket" for our example will work fine.
In "Item Name" we need to enter the bsport event we want to pull up. Our item being the add-to-cart, in the file attached higher in this form we know that the bsport event that corresponds to that is: bsport:basket:add-to-cart:pass
bsport:basket:add-to-cart:pass
Don't forget to check the "Use regular expression matching" box.
Don't forget to check the "Use regular expression matching" box.
The configuration is finished! You can save and switch to preview mode in Google Tag Manager. Google Tag Manager's preview or "debug" mode allows you to see the tags that are triggered on a website page. There is also the Chrome extension, Google Tag Assistant.

Type

The widget can be integrated on any kind of website: Wix, Wordpress, Contentsquare...
Thus, due to this diversity of environments, the integration of the widget may require a particular configuration.
If you still have a problem after consulting our widget configuration articles, we invite you to do the following checks:
- If content is displayed in your widget that it should not be (courses, workshops, course cards...), or vice versaCheck that the corresponding filters are as you expect, in your widget configuration​ Once the configuration has been changed, copy/paste the generated code back into your site​

#### If content is displayed in your widget that it should not be (courses, workshops, course cards...), or vice versa

- Check that the corresponding filters are as you expect, in your widget configuration​
Check that the corresponding filters are as you expect, in your widget configuration​
- Once the configuration has been changed, copy/paste the generated code back into your site​
Once the configuration has been changed, copy/paste the generated code back into your site​
- If the widget looks different from the preview in the back-office, or if your widget does not respond to clicksActivate the compatibility mode​ Once the configuration has been changed, copy/paste the generated code back into your site

#### If the widget looks different from the preview in the back-office, or if your widget does not respond to clicks

- Activate the compatibility mode​
Activate the compatibility mode​
- Once the configuration has been changed, copy/paste the generated code back into your site
Once the configuration has been changed, copy/paste the generated code back into your site

**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/8101909-hide-the-search-bar-and-filters-on-the-pass-page

On the page where your members can purchase passes, appointment passes, and packs, you can hide the search bar and filters.
This setting is configured independently for the website and the widget.
For the widget:
For the widget:
The setting is configured in Settings > Widgets, by selecting the Passes widget, check or uncheck the "Do not display search bar and filters" option.​​To note: To apply this change you will have to copy and paste again the code of the widget on your webpage.
For the website:
For the website:
This setting is configured in Settings > Marketplace Settings, by adding or editing a Passes tab, and checking or unchecking the "Do not display search bar and filters" option.

### Intercom
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/7973666-franchise-fees-on-sales

As a franchisor, you are likely to take a percentage of the sales made by your franchisees: use this information sheet to find out how this can be taken into account on the platform.
Step 1: Indicate franchise fees
Step 1:
You can set different fees for each of your franchisees, but you can't do this directly from the platform.
If you are interested in this feature, contact your account manager so that he can set up the percentages.
Step 2: Find the commission in the reports
Step 2:
In the purchases report (available on the Report page), you can then add the columns "Franchisee fees incl. tax" and/or "Franchisee fees excl. tax":
On each purchase, you'll find the fees owed by the franchised studios.

### Intercom
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/7973452-share-tags-across-a-franchise

You can use the Master Account to create tags that are shared between franchisees.
Step 1: Create tags on the Master Account
Step 1: Create tags on the Master Account
On the Master Account, go to the Tags tab.
As for the other tags, you can define main tags with a name, and sub tags with a name, a colour and an icon.
Step 2: Use the shared tags in the franchisee accounts
Step 2: Use the shared tags in the franchisee accounts
You cannot apply tags from the Master Account, but the tags created from the Master Account are available to be used by any franchisee.
Shared tags are applied to all franchisees in such a way that any change will be reflected across the companies. If a member is tagged with one of these, they will be tagged immediately in all companies of the franchise.
Step 3: Edit tags
Step 3: Edit tags
You can edit and delete shared tags from the Master Account, and the changes will be reflected across all franchisees.
You cannot edit nor delete the shared tags from a franchisee account.

Step 1 : Add categories on the Master Account

Step 2 : Modify the categories on the Master Account

The categories and franchisees within will appear on the franchise application in the order shown here.

### Goal
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/9424899-how-to-manage-email-campaigns-for-franchises

## Goal

Campaigns can be sent from the master account (MA), but the emails are not sent directly from the MA; they are sent from the franchisee's backoffice (BO). Understanding this logic is crucial to know who will be billed.

## Sending a Campaign

When sending a campaign from the master account, you have the choice between:
- Selecting a template
Selecting a template
- Select studios
Select studios

## Franchisee Email

The list of franchisee emails corresponds to the company email of each franchisee.
⚠️ It does not correspond to the company's sender email.

## Custom Email Domain

The custom domain must be defined in the admin under Franchisor.

## Sending Logic

The sending logic is a bit peculiar. Emails are not sent from the master account but from each of the franchisees.
💡 On the customer side, members will still see the franchisee email or custom email selected even though the email is actually sent from another franchisee.

## All Members Campaign

If the "all members" toggle is activated:
- The system will start with the first franchisee in alphabetical order.
The system will start with the first franchisee in alphabetical order.
the first franchisee in alphabetical order
- It will send the communication to all the members of this franchisee.
It will send the communication to all the members of this franchisee.
- Then, the system will proceed to the next franchisee in alphabetical order and send communications to its members, ensuring not to contact the previously contacted members again.
Then, the system will proceed to the next franchisee in alphabetical order and send communications to its members, ensuring not to contact the previously contacted members again.
- And so on...
And so on...
💡 For franchises with a shared database (all of our franchises), this means that it is always the first franchisee in alphabetical order that will send the emails and also pay for them.

## Smartlist Filtering

You can also deactivate the "send to all members" toggle to filter the franchisees and add a smartlist to filter even more within those franchisees.
- Here, there is no alphabetical order. The system arbitrarily chooses which franchisee to start with.
Here, there is no alphabetical order. The system arbitrarily chooses which franchisee to start with.
- Then it moves on to the other selected franchisees and so on, following the same rules as stated before.
Then it moves on to the other selected franchisees and so on, following the same rules as stated before.
- We do not take the intersection of smartlist/studios for the list of members to whom we are going to send the communications.Meaning that if I have Studio A and Studio B, and Member 1 is part of Studio A but not B, if those two studios are selected for the campaign, the member will receive the communication.Basically, we take all the members from all the smartlists/studios selected.
We do not take the intersection of smartlist/studios for the list of members to whom we are going to send the communications.
- Meaning that if I have Studio A and Studio B, and Member 1 is part of Studio A but not B, if those two studios are selected for the campaign, the member will receive the communication.
Meaning that if I have Studio A and Studio B, and Member 1 is part of Studio A but not B, if those two studios are selected for the campaign, the member will receive the communication.
- Basically, we take all the members from all the smartlists/studios selected.
Basically, we take all the members from all the smartlists/studios selected.
- In this case, it is difficult to know exactly which studios will send the communication. It is most likely that all studios will send something.
In this case, it is difficult to know exactly which studios will send the communication. It is most likely that all studios will send something.

## Select a Sending Studio

In the case the franchise has a shared database AND the manager wants to send a communication to all the members:
Normally, it is always the first franchisee in alphabetical order that will send the communications and pay for them. But if you want to select another one:
- In the desired franchisee, create a smartlist with all members.
In the desired franchisee, create a smartlist with all members.
- In the master account, uncheck "all members" and only activate the desired franchisee.
In the master account, uncheck "all members" and only activate the desired franchisee.
- Then select the smartlist you just created.
Then select the smartlist you just created.
- Send, and it's done. This franchisee will be the one sending the communications and paying for them.
Send, and it's done. This franchisee will be the one sending the communications and paying for them.
💡 Remember, even though all communications are coming from one franchisee, the sender email that will appear for the member is the one selected.
For additional information and support, visit our Help Center or contact your account manager.

- For the Apple Store, you can check out Creating Your Product Page, which provides guidelines and best practices for creating your app name, subtitle, icon, description, screenshots, and more. You can also check out App Store Review Guidelines, which covers the technical, content, and design criteria that Apple uses to review all apps. Some of the key points are:An app name can be up to 30 characters long and should be distinctive and avoid generic terms or names that are too similar to existing apps.A subtitle can be up to 30 characters long and should summarize your app’s functionality in a concise phrase.A full description can be up to 4000 characters long and should accurately describe your app’s functionality and content, highlight its benefits and features, and avoid misleading or irrelevant references, testimonials, or profanity.An app icon should be simple, recognizable, and avoid graphic elements that may mislead users.
For the Apple Store, you can check out Creating Your Product Page, which provides guidelines and best practices for creating your app name, subtitle, icon, description, screenshots, and more. You can also check out App Store Review Guidelines, which covers the technical, content, and design criteria that Apple uses to review all apps. Some of the key points are:
Apple Store
- An app name can be up to 30 characters long and should be distinctive and avoid generic terms or names that are too similar to existing apps.
An app name can be up to 30 characters long and should be distinctive and avoid generic terms or names that are too similar to existing apps.
- A subtitle can be up to 30 characters long and should summarize your app’s functionality in a concise phrase.
A subtitle can be up to 30 characters long and should summarize your app’s functionality in a concise phrase.
- A full description can be up to 4000 characters long and should accurately describe your app’s functionality and content, highlight its benefits and features, and avoid misleading or irrelevant references, testimonials, or profanity.
A full description can be up to 4000 characters long and should accurately describe your app’s functionality and content, highlight its benefits and features, and avoid misleading or irrelevant references, testimonials, or profanity.
- An app icon should be simple, recognizable, and avoid graphic elements that may mislead users.
An app icon should be simple, recognizable, and avoid graphic elements that may mislead users.
- For the Google Playstore, you can check out Create and set up your app, which explains how to create your app and set it up using Play Console. You can also check out Best practice for your store listing, which provides tips and examples for creating your app title, description, icon, screenshots, and more. Some of the key points are:An app title must be 30 characters or less and should be unique, accessible, and avoid common terms or profanity.A short description should convey your app’s message in 80 characters or less and give users a summary of your app’s biggest benefits.A full description should be 4000 characters or less and should describe your app’s functionality and content, highlight its features and value proposition, and avoid misleading or irrelevant references, testimonials, or profanity.An app icon should be simple, recognizable, and avoid graphic elements that may mislead users or imply store performance or promotion.
For the Google Playstore, you can check out Create and set up your app, which explains how to create your app and set it up using Play Console. You can also check out Best practice for your store listing, which provides tips and examples for creating your app title, description, icon, screenshots, and more. Some of the key points are:
Google Playstore
- An app title must be 30 characters or less and should be unique, accessible, and avoid common terms or profanity.
An app title must be 30 characters or less and should be unique, accessible, and avoid common terms or profanity.
- A short description should convey your app’s message in 80 characters or less and give users a summary of your app’s biggest benefits.
A short description should convey your app’s message in 80 characters or less and give users a summary of your app’s biggest benefits.
- A full description should be 4000 characters or less and should describe your app’s functionality and content, highlight its features and value proposition, and avoid misleading or irrelevant references, testimonials, or profanity.
A full description should be 4000 characters or less and should describe your app’s functionality and content, highlight its features and value proposition, and avoid misleading or irrelevant references, testimonials, or profanity.
- An app icon should be simple, recognizable, and avoid graphic elements that may mislead users or imply store performance or promotion.
An app icon should be simple, recognizable, and avoid graphic elements that may mislead users or imply store performance or promotion.

<aside> 📳 LOGO SPECIFICATIONS
LOGO SPECIFICATIONS
The most important specifications the client should follow about the logo are:
- The logo should be in square format (for example: 512px x 512px)
The logo should be in square format (for example: 512px x 512px)
square
- The logo must have a good resolution, that is, the image must be clear, not pixelated.
The logo must have a good resolution, that is, the image must be clear, not pixelated.
good resolution
Important: on Google Playstore we have some complaints of clients because the logo is a square with a color in the background - if the logo is square, it gets these white edges:
So the possible solutions for now that we've been given to the clients who want this is to produce a logo:
1- with a transparent or white background; or
2- with a circle logo; or
3- with a "square with rounded corners"
like this:

ℹ️ Logo specifications for the Stores :
- Google PlaystoreAccording to the Google Play icon design specifications, the icon for the app should have the these characteristics:Size: 512px x 512pxFormat: 32-bit PNGColor Space: sRGBMax File Size: 1024KBShape: Full Square—> for this, our internal system already adjusts automatically for usGoogle Play will automatically apply rounded corners and drop shadows to your icon, so I don’t need to add them myself. But we should also avoid badges and text on the icon, as they may not scale well.

### Troubleshooting D-U-N-S Verification Issues
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/9471946-how-to-obtain-a-duns-number

## Why a D-U-N-S number is required

To publish apps on Google Play Store (organizational account) and Apple App Store, a D-U-N-S Number is required. This unique 9-digit identifier, issued by Dun & Bradstreet (D&B), verifies your business’s legal identity.
Google Play Store
Apple App Store
D-U-N-S Number
Cross-platform note: A single D-U-N-S number can be used for verification on both Google and Apple platforms.
Cross-platform note:

## 1. What is a D-U-N-S Number?

1. What is a D-U-N-S Number?
A D-U-N-S number is a unique 9-digit identifier that verifies a company’s legal existence. Some companies receive a D-U-N-S automatically, while others need to request it.

## 2. Why You Need a D-U-N-S Number

2. Why You Need a D-U-N-S Number

## Google Developer Account (GDA)

- Required for organizational accounts to pass Google’s identity verification.
Required for organizational accounts to pass Google’s identity verification.
- Mandatory for publishing Android apps under a company name.
Mandatory for publishing Android apps under a company name.
- Ensures smoother, faster app deployment versus a personal account.
Ensures smoother, faster app deployment versus a personal account.
⚠️ Note for sole proprietors/freelancers: If you cannot obtain a D-U-N-S number, you may use a personal Google Developer Account, which will publish apps under your personal name.
Note for sole proprietors/freelancers:

## Apple Developer Program (ADP)

- Required for all organization-type enrollees to verify legal entity status.
Required for all organization-type enrollees to verify legal entity status.
- A D-U-N-S number ensures your account is recognized as a verified organization.
A D-U-N-S number ensures your account is recognized as a verified organization.

## 3. Steps to Obtain a D-U-N-S Number

3. Steps to Obtain a D-U-N-S Number
Follow these steps to check for and request your number.

### Step 1: Check if Your Company Already Has One

Step 1: Check if Your Company Already Has One
- If your company is registered in France, Belgium, Spain, Italy, Portugal, Luxembourg, The Netherlands, or MoroccoD&B has delegated exclusive D-U-N-S verification in these countries to its official partner VerifObtain your D-U-N-S via Verif — mandatory one-time fee of €39 (excl. VAT)
If your company is registered in France, Belgium, Spain, Italy, Portugal, Luxembourg, The Netherlands, or Morocco
If your company is registered in France, Belgium, Spain, Italy, Portugal, Luxembourg, The Netherlands, or Morocco
- D&B has delegated exclusive D-U-N-S verification in these countries to its official partner Verif
D&B has delegated exclusive D-U-N-S verification in these countries to its official partner Verif
Verif
- Obtain your D-U-N-S via Verif — mandatory one-time fee of €39 (excl. VAT)
Obtain your D-U-N-S via Verif — mandatory one-time fee of €39 (excl. VAT)
one-time fee of €39 (excl. VAT)
- If your company is located in another countryGo to the D-U-N-S Lookup Tool. Search by either:  Company registration number and country, or Legal company name and country  If the company appears: Click Email to receive your D-U-N-S number  If the company does not appear, it likely does not yet have a D-U-N-S number.
If your company is located in another country
- Go to the D-U-N-S Lookup Tool.
Go to the D-U-N-S Lookup Tool.
- Search by either:  Company registration number and country, or Legal company name and country
Search by either:
- Company registration number and country, or
Company registration number and country, or
Company registration number
- Legal company name and country
Legal company name and country
Legal company name
- If the company appears: Click Email to receive your D-U-N-S number
If the company appears:
- Click Email to receive your D-U-N-S number
Click Email to receive your D-U-N-S number
Email
- If the company does not appear, it likely does not yet have a D-U-N-S number.
If the company does not appear, it likely does not yet have a D-U-N-S number.
not yet have a D-U-N-S number.

### Step 2: Apply for a D-U-N-S Number (if not found)

Step 2: Apply for a D-U-N-S Number (if not found)
- Request a D-U-N-S number here
Request a D-U-N-S number here
- Create an account and submit your business information:Business nameAddressOwnershipIndustry

Business name

Address

Ownership

Industry
- Verification typically takes a few days, but can vary by country.
Verification typically takes a few days, but can vary by country.
a few days

Some countries may have additional costs (Italy, Spain, Morocco, etc.)
Alternative method: If the online system does not work, you can email D&B support at support@dnb.com with your company details to request a D-U-N-S number manually.

### Final Recommendation

Final Recommendation
Keep information updated: Ensure that your business information is current and accurate in the D&B database. This helps maintain your business credit file accurately.

## Troubleshooting D-U-N-S Verification Issues

If your app publishing process is blocked due to D-U-N-S verification issues, a practical workaround is available:
- Create an individual Apple Developer account initially.
Create an individual Apple Developer account initially.
- After successful setup, request Apple to upgrade it to a professional or organization account. This bypasses the D-U-N-S verification at the onset, simplifying the process for businesses.
After successful setup, request Apple to upgrade it to a professional or organization account. This bypasses the D-U-N-S verification at the onset, simplifying the process for businesses.
⚠️ Troubleshooting tip:
Troubleshooting tip:
- If Apple does not recognize your D-U-N-S number:Double-check your company’s legal name and country.Ensure the D-U-N-S number is active and corresponds to your legal entity.Contact Apple Developer Support if verification fails.
If Apple does not recognize your D-U-N-S number:
- Double-check your company’s legal name and country.
Double-check your company’s legal name and country.
- Ensure the D-U-N-S number is active and corresponds to your legal entity.
Ensure the D-U-N-S number is active and corresponds to your legal entity.
- Contact Apple Developer Support if verification fails.
Contact Apple Developer Support if verification fails.

et us know!

### Intercom
**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/9930184-google-play-console-how-to-verify-android-device

## Why is Google requesting Android mobile device verification?

Why is Google requesting Android mobile device verification?
- Google requires developers to verify that they have access to an Android mobile device in order to publish apps on Google Play. This ensures that developers have the necessary tools to test their apps and to maintain app quality and security standards. Only the account owner of the Google Developer Account (GDA) can complete this verification.
Google requires developers to verify that they have access to an Android mobile device in order to publish apps on Google Play. This ensures that developers have the necessary tools to test their apps and to maintain app quality and security standards. Only the account owner of the Google Developer Account (GDA) can complete this verification.
Only the account owner

## Step-by-Step Guide for Verification:

Step-by-Step Guide for Verification:
1. Download the Google Play Console App on an Android Device:
1. Download the Google Play Console App on an Android Device:
- If the company owner (the owner of the GDA) doesn't already have the Google Play Console app installed on their Android device, they will need to:Scan the QR code shown in the image provided, orSearch for the Google Play Console app on the Google Play Store and download it manually.
If the company owner (the owner of the GDA) doesn't already have the Google Play Console app installed on their Android device, they will need to:
- Scan the QR code shown in the image provided, or
Scan the QR code shown in the image provided, or
- Search for the Google Play Console app on the Google Play Store and download it manually.
Search for the Google Play Console app on the Google Play Store and download it manually.
2. Sign in to the Google Play Console App:
2. Sign in to the Google Play Console App:
- Once the app is installed, they must sign in using the same Google account they are using for the Google Developer Account (the same email as shown in the instructions, e.g., google-developer-android-1@bsport.io).
Once the app is installed, they must sign in using the same Google account they are using for the Google Developer Account (the same email as shown in the instructions, e.g., google-developer-android-1@bsport.io).
Once the app is installed, they must sign in using the same Google account they are using for the Google Developer Account (the same email as shown in the instructions, e.g., google-developer-android-1@bsport.io).
3. Verify Access to the Developer Account:
3. Verify Access to the Developer Account:
- After signing in, select the appropriate Developer Account (in this case, it should be the company account).
After signing in, select the appropriate Developer Account (in this case, it should be the company account).
After signing in, select the appropriate Developer Account (in this case, it should be the company account).
- Follow the instructions that appear in the app to complete the verification process on the Android device.
Follow the instructions that appear in the app to complete the verification process on the Android device.

## What to Do if the Client Does Not Have an Android Device:

What to Do if the Client Does Not Have an Android Device:
If the client (the company owner) does not have access to an Android device, there are a few options:
1. Use an Android Device Temporarily:
1. Use an Android Device Temporarily:
- The owner can borrow an Android device temporarily to complete the verification.
The owner can borrow an Android device temporarily to complete the verification.
- They would need to install the Google Play Console app, sign in with their account, and follow the same steps outlined above.
They would need to install the Google Play Console app, sign in with their account, and follow the same steps outlined above.
2. Allow the Account Manager to Verify on Your Behalf:
2. Allow the Account Manager to Verify on Your Behalf:
- The owner can provide their Google Developer Account credentials to their bsport account manager, who can complete the verification on their behalf using an Android device.
The owner can provide their Google Developer Account credentials to their bsport account manager, who can complete the verification on their behalf using an Android device.
3. Purchase an Android Device:
3. Purchase an Android Device:
- If the owner frequently needs to manage Android apps, it might be worth investing in a basic Android device to handle verifications, testing, and development.
If the owner frequently needs to manage Android apps, it might be worth investing in a basic Android device to handle verifications, testing, and development.
4. Contact Google Support:
4. Contact Google Support:
- If none of the above options are feasible, the client can contact Google Support and explain that they do not have access to an Android device. Google may provide an alternative verification method or offer further guidance.
If none of the above options are feasible, the client can contact Google Support and explain that they do not have access to an Android device. Google may provide an alternative verification method or offer further guidance.
5. Emulate Android:
5. Emulate Android:
- Another option is to use an Android emulator on a computer. Tools like Android Studio provide virtual devices that could potentially be used to run the Play Console app. However, this approach is less reliable and may not be officially supported by Google for verification purposes.
Another option is to use an Android emulator on a computer. Tools like Android Studio provide virtual devices that could potentially be used to run the Play Console app. However, this approach is less reliable and may not be officially supported by Google for verification purposes.

The owner can borrow an Android device temporarily to complete the verification.
- They would need to install the Google Play Console app, sign in with their account, and follow the same steps outlined above.
They would need to install the Google Play Console app, sign in with their account, and follow the same steps outlined above.
2. Allow the Account Manager to Verify on Your Behalf:
2. Allow the Account Manager to Verify on Your Behalf:
- The owner can provide their Google Developer Account credentials to their bsport account manager, who can complete the verification on their behalf using an Android device.
The owner can provide their Google Developer Account credentials to their bsport account manager, who can complete the verification on their behalf using an Android device.
3. Purchase an Android Device:
3. Purchase an Android Device:
- If the owner frequently needs to manage Android apps, it might be worth investing in a basic Android device to handle verifications, testing, and development.
If the owner frequently needs to manage Android apps, it might be worth investing in a basic Android device to handle verifications, testing, and development.
4. Contact Google Support:
4. Contact Google Support:
- If none of the above options are feasible, the client can contact Google Support and explain that they do not have access to an Android device. Google may provide an alternative verification method or offer further guidance.
If none of the above options are feasible, the client can contact Google Support and explain that they do not have access to an Android device. Google may provide an alternative verification method or offer further guidance.
5. Emulate Android:
5. Emulate Android:
- Another option is to use an Android emulator on a computer. Tools like Android Studio provide virtual devices that could potentially be used to run the Play Console app. However, this approach is less reliable and may not be officially supported by Google for verification purposes.
Another option is to use an Android emulator on a computer. Tools like Android Studio provide virtual devices that could potentially be used to run the Play Console app. However, this approach is less reliable and may not be officially supported by Google for verification purposes.

If your organization uses a custom domain (e.g. from GoDaddy, Squarespace, etc.), you’ll need to verify it in the Google Play Console to link your website to your app.
custom domain
verify it in the Google Play Console

## 🎥 First: Watch the Video Tutorial

## 📘 Then: Follow the Step-by-Step Instructions Below

Use the instructions below to complete the domain verification in Google Play Console.

## Step 1: Log in to Google Play Console

Step 1: Log in to Google Play Console
- Once logged in, you will see a message prompting you to verify your organization’s website.​
Once logged in, you will see a message prompting you to verify your organization’s website.​
- On the right side, click on the Details option.​
On the right side, click on the Details option.​
Details
- Scroll down until you see two options: Go to Search Console and Verify Website.​
Scroll down until you see two options: Go to Search Console and Verify Website.​
Go to Search Console
Verify Website
- First, click on Go to Search Console.​
First, click on Go to Search Console.​
Go to Search Console

## Step 2: Access Google Search Console

Step 2: Access Google Search Console
- You will be redirected to the Google Search Console page.​
You will be redirected to the Google Search Console page.​
- Click Start now.​
Click Start now.​
Start now
- Again, you will be directed to another page.​
Again, you will be directed to another page.​
- Before proceeding, check the email address in the top right corner of the page.​
Before proceeding, check the email address in the top right corner of the page.​
- Make sure this email matches the email used to create your Google Play Console account.​
Make sure this email matches the email used to create your Google Play Console account.​
- To verify which email you used for Google Play Console, go to Users and Permissions in your Play Console.
To verify which email you used for Google Play Console, go to Users and Permissions in your Play Console.
Users and Permissions

## Step 3: Choose Verification Method

Step 3: Choose Verification Method
- In Google Search Console, you will see two verification options:​Domain​URL Prefix
In Google Search Console, you will see two verification options:​
- Domain​
Domain​
Domain​
- URL Prefix
URL Prefix
URL Prefix
- Since you have a custom domain, choose the Domain option.
Since you have a custom domain, choose the Domain option.
Domain
- Enter your domain name without the “https://” or “www”. For example: example.com.
Enter your domain name without the “https://” or “www”. For example: example.com.
- Click Continue.​
Click Continue.​
Continue

## Step 4: Add TXT Record to Your DNS

Step 4: Add TXT Record to Your DNS
- You will get a TXT record code to add to your domain’s DNS settings.​
You will get a TXT record code to add to your domain’s DNS settings.​
- If your domain provider is GoDaddy, you may be able to do an automatic verification.​
If your domain provider is GoDaddy, you may be able to do an automatic verification.​
- Otherwise, select Any DNS provider.​
Otherwise, select Any DNS provider.​
Any DNS provider
- Copy the TXT record provided (it will look like a string of letters and numbers).​
Copy the TXT record provided (it will look like a string of letters and numbers).​

## Step 5: Add TXT Record to Your Domain Provider

Step 5: Add TXT Record to Your Domain Provider
- Open a new browser tab and go to your domain provider’s help center (e.g., GoDaddy Help Center, Squarespace Help Center).​
Open a new browser tab and go to your domain provider’s help center (e.g., GoDaddy Help Center, Squarespace Help Center).​
- Search for “Add TXT record”.​
Search for “Add TXT record”.​
“Add TXT record”
- Follow the instructions to add the TXT record to your DNS settings.​
Follow the instructions to add the TXT record to your DNS settings.​
- Usually, you will:​Go to your domain management or DNS settings page​Add a new TXT record​Paste the copied TXT record into the value/points-to field​Leave the name/host field empty or use “@” (depending on provider)​Save the record​​
Usually, you will:​
- Go to your domain management or DNS settings page​
Go to your domain management or DNS settings page​
- Add a new TXT record​
Add a new TXT record​
- Paste the copied TXT record into the value/points-to field​
Paste the copied TXT record into the value/points-to field​
- Leave the name/host field empty or use “@” (depending on provider)​
Leave the name/host field empty or use “@” (depending on provider)​
- Save the record​​
Save the record​​

## Step 6: Wait for DNS Propagation

Step 6: Wait for DNS Propagation
- DNS changes can take anywhere from a few minutes to 48 hours to propagate globally.​
DNS changes can take anywhere from a few minutes to 48 hours to propagate globally.​
- Do not panic if verification fails immediately.​
Do not panic if verification fails immediately.​
- Some providers may take up to 72 hours.​
Some providers may take up to 72 hours.​
- After waiting, return to Google Search Console and click Verify.​​
After waiting, return to Google Search Console and click Verify.​​
Verify

## Step 7: Confirm Website Ownership in Search Console

Step 7: Confirm Website Ownership in Search Console
- If verification is successful, you will see a green confirmation message.
If verification is successful, you will see a green confirmation message.
- This means your ownership is validated.​
This means your ownership is validated.​
- Note the difference between validation and verification — for Google Search Console, validation confirms ownership.​​
Note the difference between validation and verification — for Google Search Console, validation confirms ownership.​​

## Step 8: Finalize Verification in Google Play Console

Step 8: Finalize Verification in Google Play Console
- Return to Google Play Console.​
Return to Google Play Console.​
- Click on Verify Website.​
Click on Verify Website.​
Verify Website
- Scroll down to the Organization Website section.​
Scroll down to the Organization Website section.​
Organization Website
- Make sure the website URL matches exactly the domain you verified in Search Console.​
Make sure the website URL matches exactly the domain you verified in Search Console.​
- Ensure the email on the top right matches the one used in Google Play Console.​
Ensure the email on the top right matches the one used in Google Play Console.​
- Click Send Verification Link.​
Click Send Verification Link.​
Send Verification Link
- This option is only available if you are the account owner.​​
This option is only available if you are the account owner.​​

## Troubleshooting Tips

Troubleshooting Tips
- If you see an error, double-check that:​The emails used in Google Search Console and Google Play Console are the same.​The website URLs match exactly (no missing letters or typos).​
If you see an error, double-check that:​
- The emails used in Google Search Console and Google Play Console are the same.​
The emails used in Google Search Console and Google Play Console are the same.​
- The website URLs match exactly (no missing letters or typos).​
The website URLs match exactly (no missing letters or typos).​
- If needed, delete the organization website URL and re-enter it correctly.​
If needed, delete the organization website URL and re-enter it correctly.​
- If verification still fails, wait 48-72 hours and try again.​​
If verification still fails, wait 48-72 hours and try again.​​

**Source:** https://intercom.help/bsport-helpcenter/en/articles/12034682-tracking-app-downloads-in-your-google-and-apple-developer-accounts

If you already have access to your Google Developer Account (GDA) and Apple Developer Account, you can track the number of app downloads directly through each platform.
Google Developer Account (GDA)
Apple Developer Account

## Google Play (GDA)

- Log into your Google Play Console and select your branded app from the list.
Log into your Google Play Console and select your branded app from the list.
Google Play Console
- From the left-hand menu, click Statistics.
From the left-hand menu, click Statistics.
Statistics
- Remove any pre-selected countries (click the ❌ next to them) and select the timeframe you want to analyze.
Remove any pre-selected countries (click the ❌ next to them) and select the timeframe you want to analyze.
timeframe
- Under Users > User acquisition > New users, you’ll see:   New user installs → the number of first-time downloads. In the Configure report section Change the view to Cumulative under Metric calculation and click Save. Total installs → the overall number of downloads (including re-installs). 👉 You should use: per interval (to see raw numbers). ⚠️ Avoid using 30 days rolling average for reporting installs/downloads, since it’s designed more for trend analysis and won’t give you the real totals.
Under Users > User acquisition > New users, you’ll see:
Users > User acquisition > New users
- New user installs → the number of first-time downloads. In the Configure report section Change the view to Cumulative under Metric calculation and click Save.

New user installs
Configure report
Cumulative
Save
- Total installs → the overall number of downloads (including re-installs). 👉 You should use: per interval (to see raw numbers). ⚠️ Avoid using 30 days rolling average for reporting installs/downloads, since it’s designed more for trend analysis and won’t give you the real totals.

Total installs

per interval
